             'class="infobox-label" scope="row"><a href="Medication" '
             'title="Medication">Medication</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a '

             'title="Corticosteroid">steroids</a></td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row">Frequency</th><td class="infobox-data">213\xa0million (2021);<br/> 2.7% '
             'of global population</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row">Deaths</th><td class="infobox-data">3.65\xa0million (2021);<br/> 5% of '
             'global deaths</td></tr></tbody></table> <p><b>Chronic obstructive pulmonary '
             'disease</b> (<b>COPD</b>) is a type of progressive <a '
             'href="Obstructive_lung_disease" title="Obstructive lung disease">lung disease</a> '
             'characterized by chronic respiratory symptoms and airflow limitation. <a '
             'href="Global_Initiative_for_Chronic_Obstructive_Lung_Disease" title="Global '
             'Initiative for Chronic Obstructive Lung Disease">GOLD</a> defines COPD as a '
             'heterogeneous lung condition characterized by chronic respiratory symptoms (<a '
             'href="Shortness_of_breath" title="Shortness of breath">shortness of breath</a>, <a '
             'href="Cough" title="Cough">cough</a>, sputum production or <a '
             'href="Acute_exacerbation_of_chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Acute '
             'exacerbation of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease">exacerbations</a>) due to '
             'abnormalities of the airways (<a href="Bronchitis" '
             'title="Bronchitis">bronchitis</a>, <a href="Bronchiolitis" '
             'title="Bronchiolitis">bronchiolitis</a>) or <a href="Alveolus" '
             'title="Alveolus">alveoli</a> (<a href="Emphysema" title="Emphysema">emphysema</a>) '
             'that cause persistent, often progressive, <a href="Obstructive_lung_disease" '
             'title="Obstructive lung disease">airflow obstruction</a>. </p><p>The main symptoms '
             'of COPD include shortness of breath and a <a href="Cough" title="Cough">cough</a>, '
             'which may or may not produce <a href="Mucus" title="Mucus">mucus</a>. COPD <a '
             'href="Progressive_disease" title="Progressive disease">progressively worsens</a>, '
             'with <a href="Activities_of_daily_living" title="Activities of daily '
             'living">everyday activities</a> such as walking or dressing becoming difficult. '
             'While COPD is incurable, it is preventable and treatable. The two most common types '
             'of COPD are <a href="Emphysema" title="Emphysema">emphysema</a> and <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Chronic_bronchitis" title="Chronic bronchitis">chronic '
             'bronchitis</a>, and have been the two classic COPD <a '
             'href="Phenotype_(clinical_medicine)" title="Phenotype (clinical '
             'medicine)">phenotypes</a>. However, this basic dogma has been challenged as varying '
             'degrees of co-existing emphysema, chronic bronchitis, and potentially significant '
             'vascular diseases have all been acknowledged in those with COPD, giving rise to the '
             'classification of other phenotypes or subtypes. </p><p>Emphysema is defined as '
             'enlarged airspaces (<a href="Pulmonary_alveolus" title="Pulmonary '
             'alveolus">alveoli</a>) whose <a class="mw-redirect" href="Alveolar_wall" '
             'title="Alveolar wall">walls</a> have broken down, resulting in permanent damage to '
             'the lung tissue. Chronic bronchitis is defined as a productive cough that is present '
             'for at least three months each year for two years. Both of these conditions can '
             'exist without airflow limitations when they are not classed as COPD. Emphysema is '
             'just one of the structural abnormalities that can limit airflow and can exist '
             'without airflow limitation in a significant number of people. Chronic bronchitis '
             'does not always result in airflow limitation. However, in young adults with chronic '
             'bronchitis who smoke, the risk of developing COPD is high. Many definitions of COPD '
             'in the past included emphysema and chronic bronchitis, but these have never been '
             'included in <a href="Global_Initiative_for_Chronic_Obstructive_Lung_Disease" '
             'title="Global Initiative for Chronic Obstructive Lung Disease">GOLD report</a> '
             'definitions. Emphysema and chronic bronchitis remain the predominant phenotypes of '
             'COPD, but there is often overlap between them, and several other phenotypes have '
             'also been described. COPD and <a href="Asthma" title="Asthma">asthma</a> may coexist '
             'and converge in some individuals. COPD is associated with low-grade systemic '
             'inflammation. </p><p>The most common cause of COPD is <a href="Tobacco_smoking" '
             'title="Tobacco smoking">tobacco smoking</a>. Other <a href="Risk_factor" title="Risk '
             'factor">risk factors</a> include indoor and <a href="Air_pollution" title="Air '
             'pollution">outdoor air pollution</a> including dust, exposure to occupational '
             'irritants such as dust from grains, <a href="Cadmium_poisoning" title="Cadmium '
             'poisoning">cadmium dust or fumes</a>, and <a href="Genetics" '
             'title="Genetics">genetics</a>, such as <a href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin_deficiency" '
             'title="Alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency">alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency</a>. In <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Developing_countries" title="Developing '
             'countries">developing countries</a>, common sources of household air pollution are '
             'the use of coal and biomass such as wood and <a href="Dry_dung_fuel" title="Dry dung '
             'fuel">dry dung</a> as fuel for cooking and heating. The diagnosis is based on poor '
             'airflow as measured by <a href="Spirometry" title="Spirometry">spirometry</a>. '
             '</p><p>Most cases of COPD can be prevented by reducing exposure to risk factors such '
             'as smoking and indoor and outdoor pollutants. While treatment can slow worsening, '
             'there is no conclusive evidence that any medications can change the long-term '
             'decline in lung function. COPD treatments include <a href="Smoking_cessation" '
             'title="Smoking cessation">smoking cessation</a>, <a href="Vaccination" '
             'title="Vaccination">vaccinations</a>, <a href="Pulmonary_rehabilitation" '
             'title="Pulmonary rehabilitation">pulmonary rehabilitation</a>, <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Metered_dose_inhaler" title="Metered dose '
             'inhaler">inhaled</a> <a href="Bronchodilator" '
             'title="Bronchodilator">bronchodilators</a> and <a href="Corticosteroid" '
             'title="Corticosteroid">corticosteroids</a>. Some people may benefit from long-term '
             '<a href="Oxygen_therapy" title="Oxygen therapy">oxygen therapy</a>, <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Lung_volume_reduction" title="Lung volume reduction">lung '
             'volume reduction</a> and <a href="Lung_transplantation" title="Lung '
             'transplantation">lung transplantation</a>. In those who have periods of <a '
             'href="Acute_exacerbation_of_chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Acute '
             'exacerbation of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease">acute worsening</a>, '
             'increased use of medications, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Antibiotics" '
             'title="Antibiotics">antibiotics</a>, corticosteroids and hospitalization may be '
             'needed. </p><p>As of 2021, COPD affected about 213\xa0million people (2.7% of the '
             'global population). It typically occurs in males and females over the age of 35–40. '
             'In 2021, COPD caused 3.65\xa0million deaths. Almost 90% of COPD deaths in those '
             'under 70 years of age occur in <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Low_and_middle_income_countries" title="Low and middle income countries">low '
             'and middle income countries</a>. In 2021, it was the fourth biggest cause of death, '
             'responsible for approximately 5% of total deaths. The number of deaths is projected '
             'to increase further because of continued exposure to risk factors and an aging '
             'population. In the United States, costs of the disease were estimated in 2010 at '
             '$50\xa0billion, most of which is due to exacerbation. </p> <div '
             'class="toclimit-3"><meta property="mw:PageProp/toc"/></div> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Signs_and_symptoms">Signs and symptoms</h2></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="2372" data-file-width="1390" decoding="async" height="427" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Symptoms_of_COPD.svg.png" width="250"/><figcaption><a '
             'href="Signs_and_symptoms" title="Signs and symptoms">Signs and symptoms</a> of '
             'stages of COPD</figcaption></figure> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Shortness_of_breath">Shortness of breath</h3></div> <p>A <a '
             'href="Signs_and_symptoms#Cardinal_signs_and_symptoms" title="Signs and '
             'symptoms">cardinal symptom</a> of COPD is the chronic and progressive <a '
             'href="Shortness_of_breath" title="Shortness of breath">shortness of breath</a> which '
             'is most characteristic of the condition. Shortness of breath (breathlessness) is '
             'often the most distressing symptom, responsible for the associated anxiety and level '
             'of disability experienced. Symptoms of <a class="mw-redirect" href="Wheezing" '
             'title="Wheezing">wheezing</a> and chest tightness associated with breathlessness can '
             'be variable over the course of a day or between days and are not always present. '
             'Chest tightness often follows exertion. Many people with more advanced COPD <a '
             'href="Pursed-lip_breathing" title="Pursed-lip breathing">breathe through pursed '
             'lips</a>, which can improve shortness of breath. Shortness of breath is often '
             'responsible for reduced physical activity, and low levels of physical activity are '
             'associated with worse outcomes. In severe and very severe cases there may be <a '
             'href="Fatigue" title="Fatigue">constant tiredness</a>, weight loss, muscle loss and '
             '<a class="mw-redirect" href="Anorexia" title="Anorexia">anorexia</a>. People with '
             'COPD often have increased breathlessness and frequent <a href="Common_cold" '
             'title="Common cold">colds</a> before seeking treatment. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Cough">Cough</h3></div> <p>The most often first symptom of COPD '
             'is a chronic cough, which may or may not be productive of <a href="Mucus" '
             'title="Mucus">mucus</a> as <a href="Phlegm" title="Phlegm">phlegm</a>. Phlegm '
             'coughed up as <a href="Sputum" title="Sputum">sputum</a> can be intermittent and may '
             'be swallowed or spat out depending on social or cultural factors, and is therefore '
             'not always easy to evaluate. However, an accompanying productive cough is only seen '
             'in up to 30% of cases. Sometimes, limited airflow may develop in the absence of a '
             'cough. Symptoms are usually worse in the morning. </p><p>A chronic <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Productive_cough" title="Productive cough">productive '
             'cough</a> is the result of <a class="mw-redirect" href="Mucus_hypersecretion" '
             'title="Mucus hypersecretion">mucus hypersecretion</a>, and when it persists for more '
             'than three months each year for at least two years, it is defined as <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Chronic_bronchitis" title="Chronic bronchitis">chronic '
             'bronchitis</a>. Chronic bronchitis can occur before the restricted airflow '
             'diagnostic of COPD. Some people with COPD attribute the symptoms to the consequences '
             'of smoking. In severe COPD, vigorous coughing may lead to <a href="Rib_fracture" '
             'title="Rib fracture">rib fractures</a> or to <a href="Syncope_(medicine)" '
             'title="Syncope (medicine)">a\xa0brief loss of consciousness</a>. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Exacerbations">Exacerbations</h2></div> <p>An '
             '<a href="Acute_exacerbation_of_chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Acute '
             'exacerbation of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease">acute exacerbation</a> is a '
             'sudden worsening of <a href="Signs_and_symptoms" title="Signs and symptoms">signs '
             'and symptoms</a> that lasts for several days. The key symptom is increased '
             'breathlessness; other more pronounced symptoms are of excessive mucus, increased '
             'cough and wheeze. A commonly found sign is <a href="Air_trapping" title="Air '
             'trapping">air trapping</a>, giving difficulty in complete <a href="Exhalation" '
             'title="Exhalation">exhalation</a>. The usual cause of an exacerbation is a <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Viral_infection" title="Viral infection">viral '
             'infection</a>, most often the <a href="Common_cold" title="Common cold">common '
             'cold</a>. The common cold is usually associated with the winter months but can occur '
             'at any time. Other <a class="mw-redirect" href="Upper_respiratory_infection" '
             'title="Upper respiratory infection">respiratory infections</a> may be bacterial or '
             'in combination sometimes secondary to a viral infection. The most common bacterial '
             'infection is caused by <i><a href="Haemophilus_influenzae" title="Haemophilus '
             'influenzae">Haemophilus influenzae</a></i>. Other risks include exposure to tobacco '
             'smoke (active and <a href="Passive_smoking" title="Passive smoking">passive</a>) and '
             'environmental pollutants\xa0– both indoor and outdoor. During the <a '
             'href="COVID-19_pandemic" title="COVID-19 pandemic">COVID-19 pandemic</a>, hospital '
             'admissions for COPD exacerbations sharply decreased which may be attributable to '
             'reduction of emissions and cleaner air. There has also been a marked decrease in the '
             'number of cold and flu infections during this time. </p><p>Smoke from wildfires is '
             'proving an increasing risk in many parts of the world, and government agencies have '
             'published protective advice on their websites. In the US the EPA advises that the '
             'use of dust masks do not give protection from the fine particles in wildfires and '
             'instead advise the use of well-fitting <a href="Respirator" '
             'title="Respirator"><i>particulate masks</i></a>. This same advice is offered in '
             'Canada and Australia to the effects of their forest fires. </p><p>The number of '
             'exacerbations is not seen to relate to any stage of the disease; those with two or '
             'more a year are classed as <i>frequent exacerbators</i> and these lead to a '
             'worsening in the disease progression. <a href="Frailty_syndrome" title="Frailty '
             'syndrome">Frailty</a> in ageing increases exacerbations and hospitalization. '
             '</p><p>Acute exacerbations in COPD are often unexplained and thought to have many '
             'causes other than infections. A study has emphasized the possibility of a <a '
             'href="Pulmonary_embolism" title="Pulmonary embolism">pulmonary embolism</a> as '
             'sometimes being responsible in these cases. Signs can include <a href="Pleurisy" '
             'title="Pleurisy">pleuritic</a> <a href="Chest_pain" title="Chest pain">chest '
             'pain</a> and <a href="Heart_failure" title="Heart failure">heart failure</a> without '
             'signs of infection. Such emboli could respond to <a href="Anticoagulant" '
             'title="Anticoagulant">anticoagulants</a>. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Other_conditions">Other conditions</h2></div> <p>COPD often '
             'occurs along with several other conditions (<a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Comorbidities" title="Comorbidities">comorbidities</a>) due in part to shared '
             'risk factors. Common comorbidities include <a href="Cardiovascular_disease" '
             'title="Cardiovascular disease">cardiovascular disease</a>, <a href="Skeletal_muscle" '
             'title="Skeletal muscle">skeletal muscle</a> dysfunction, <a '
             'href="Metabolic_syndrome" title="Metabolic syndrome">metabolic syndrome</a>, <a '
             'href="Osteoporosis" title="Osteoporosis">osteoporosis</a>, <a '
             'href="Major_depressive_disorder" title="Major depressive disorder">depression</a>, '
             '<a href="Anxiety_disorder" title="Anxiety disorder">anxiety</a>, <a href="Asthma" '
             'title="Asthma">asthma</a> and <a href="Lung_cancer" title="Lung cancer">lung '
             'cancer</a>. <a href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin_deficiency" title="Alpha-1 antitrypsin '
             'deficiency">Alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency</a> (A1AD) is an important risk factor '
             'for COPD. It is advised that everybody with COPD be screened for A1AD. <a '
             'href="Metabolic_syndrome" title="Metabolic syndrome">Metabolic syndrome</a> has been '
             'seen to affect up to fifty percent of those with COPD and significantly affects the '
             'outcomes. When comorbid with COPD there is more systemic inflammation. It is not '
             'known if it co-exists with COPD or develops as a consequence of the pathology. '
             'Metabolic syndrome on its own has a high rate of morbidity and mortality, and this '
             'rate is amplified when comorbid with COPD. <a href="Tuberculosis" '
             'title="Tuberculosis">Tuberculosis</a> is a risk factor for the development of COPD, '
             'and is also a potential comorbidity. Most people with COPD die from comorbidities '
             'and not from respiratory problems. </p><p>Anxiety and depression are often '
             'complications of COPD. Other complications include reduced <a href="Quality_of_life" '
             'title="Quality of life">quality of life</a> and increased disability, <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Cor_pulmonale" title="Cor pulmonale">cor pulmonale</a>, '
             'frequent chest infections including <a href="Pneumonia" '
             'title="Pneumonia">pneumonia</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Secondary_polycythemia" title="Secondary polycythemia">secondary '
             'polycythemia</a>, <a href="Respiratory_failure#Type_2" title="Respiratory '
             'failure">respiratory failure</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Secondary_spontaneous_pneumothorax" title="Secondary spontaneous '
             'pneumothorax">pneumothorax</a>, lung cancer, and <a href="Cachexia" '
             'title="Cachexia">cachexia</a> (muscle wasting). </p><p>Along with these '
             'complications, there is an associated risk of developing <a '
             'href="Pulmonary_hypertension" title="Pulmonary hypertension">pulmonary '
             'hypertension</a>. The estimated prevalence of pulmonary hypertension complicating '
             'COPD was reported at 39% in a meta-analysis. Of the people with COPD listed for lung '
             'transplantation, 82% were documented as having pulmonary hypertension via right '
             'heart catheterization, noting a mean pulmonary arterial pressure greater than 20mm '
             'Hg. Despite pulmonary hypertension being relatively rare in people with COPD, mild '
             'elevations of pulmonary arterial pressure can lead to worse outcomes, including risk '
             'of death. </p><p><a href="Cognitive_impairment" title="Cognitive '
             'impairment">Cognitive impairment</a> is common in those with COPD, as it is for '
             'other lung conditions that affect airflow. Cognitive impairment is associated with '
             'the declining ability to cope with the basic <a href="Activities_of_daily_living" '
             'title="Activities of daily living">activities of daily living</a>. </p><p>It is '
             'unclear if those with COPD are at greater risk of contracting <a href="COVID-19" '
             'title="COVID-19">COVID-19</a>, though if infected, they are at risk of '
             'hospitalization and developing severe COVID-19. However, laboratory and clinical '
             'studies are showing a possibility of certain <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Inhaled_corticosteroids" title="Inhaled corticosteroids">inhaled '
             'corticosteroids</a> for COPD providing a protective role against COVID-19.  '
             'Differentiating COVID-19 symptoms from an exacerbation is difficult; mild <a '
             'href="Prodrome" title="Prodrome">prodromal symptoms</a> may delay its recognition, '
             'and where they include loss of taste or smell, COVID-19 is to be suspected. </p> '
             '<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Definition">Definition</h3></div> '
             '<p>Many definitions of COPD in the past have included chronic bronchitis and <a '
             'href="Emphysema" title="Emphysema">emphysema</a>, but these have never been included '
             'in <a href="Global_Initiative_for_Chronic_Obstructive_Lung_Disease" title="Global '
             'Initiative for Chronic Obstructive Lung Disease">GOLD report</a> definitions. '
             'Emphysema is defined as enlarged airspaces (<a href="Pulmonary_alveolus" '
             'title="Pulmonary alveolus">alveoli</a>) whose walls break down resulting in '
             'permanent damage to the lung tissue and is just one of the structural abnormalities '
             'that can limit airflow. The condition can exist without airflow limitation, but '
             'commonly it does. Chronic bronchitis is defined as a <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Productive_cough" title="Productive cough">productive cough</a> that is '
             'present for at least three months each year for two years, but does not always '
             'result in airflow limitation, although the risk of developing COPD is great. These '
             'older definitions grouped the two types as <i>type A</i> and <i>type B</i>. Type A '
             'were emphysema types known as <b>pink puffers</b> due to their pink complexion, fast '
             'breathing rate and pursed lips. Type B were chronic bronchitic types referred to as '
             '<b>blue bloaters</b> due to low oxygen levels causing a <a href="Cyanosis" '
             'title="Cyanosis">bluish color to the skin and lips</a> and swollen ankles. These '
             'differences were suggested to be due to the presence or not of <a '
             'href="Collateral_ventilation" title="Collateral ventilation">collateral '
             'ventilation</a>, evident in emphysema and lacking in chronic bronchitis. This '
             'terminology was no longer accepted as useful, as most people with COPD have a '
             'combination of both emphysema and airway disease. These are now recognized as the '
             'two major phenotypes of COPD — the emphysematous phenotype and the chronic '
             'bronchitic phenotype. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Subtypes">Subtypes</h2></div> <p>It has since been recognized that COPD is more '
             'complex, with a diverse group of disorders of differing risk factors and clinical '
             'courses that have resulted in several subtypes or phenotypes of COPD being accepted '
             'and proposed. The two classic emphysematous and chronic bronchitic <a '
             'href="Phenotype_(clinical_medicine)" title="Phenotype (clinical '
             'medicine)">phenotypes</a> are fundamentally different conditions with unique '
             'underlying mechanisms. Another subtype of COPD, categorized by some as a separate '
             'clinical entity, is <a href="Asthma-COPD_overlap" title="Asthma-COPD '
             'overlap">asthma-COPD overlap</a>, which is a condition sharing clinical features of '
             'both <a href="Asthma" title="Asthma">asthma</a> and COPD. Spirometry measures are '
             'inadequate for defining phenotypes and chest X-ray, CT and MRI scans have been '
             'mostly employed. Most cases of COPD are diagnosed at a late stage, and the use of '
             'imaging methods would allow earlier detection and treatment. </p><p>The '
             'identification and recognition of different phenotypes can guide appropriate '
             'treatment approaches. For example, the <a href="PDE4_inhibitor" title="PDE4 '
             'inhibitor">PDE4 inhibitor</a> <a href="Roflumilast" '
             'title="Roflumilast">roflumilast</a> is targeted at the chronic-bronchitic phenotype. '
             '</p><p>Two inflammatory phenotypes show a phenotype stability: the <a '
             'href="Neutrophil" title="Neutrophil">neutrophilic</a> inflammatory phenotype and the '
             '<a href="Eosinophil" title="Eosinophil">eosinophilic</a> inflammatory phenotype. <a '
             'href="Mepolizumab" title="Mepolizumab">Mepolizumab</a>, a <a '
             'href="Monoclonal_antibody" title="Monoclonal antibody">monoclonal antibody</a>, has '
             'been shown to have benefit in treating the eosinophilic inflammatory type rather '
             'than the use of oral corticosteroids, but further studies have been called for. '
             '</p><p>Another recognized phenotype is the frequent exacerbator. The frequent '
             'exacerbator has two or more exacerbations a year, has a poor prognosis, and is '
             'described as a moderately stable phenotype. </p><p>A pulmonary vascular COPD '
             'phenotype has been described due to cardiovascular dysfunction. A molecular '
             'phenotype of <a class="mw-redirect" href="CFTR" title="CFTR">CFTR</a> dysfunction is '
             'shared with <a href="Cystic_fibrosis" title="Cystic fibrosis">cystic fibrosis</a>. A '
             'combined phenotype of chronic bronchitis and <a href="Bronchiectasis" '
             'title="Bronchiectasis">bronchiectasis</a> has been described, with a difficulty '
             'noted in determining the best treatment. </p><p>The only genotype is the <a '
             'href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin_deficiency" title="Alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency">alpha-1 '
             'antitrypsin deficiency</a> (AATD) genetic subtype, and this has a specific '
             'treatment. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Cause">Cause</h2></div> '
             '<p>The most common cause of the development of COPD is the exposure to harmful '
             'particles or gases, including <a href="Tobacco_smoke" title="Tobacco smoke">tobacco '
             'smoke</a>, that irritate the lung, causing inflammation that interacts with several '
             'host factors. Such exposure needs to be significant or long-term. The greatest risk '
             'factor for the development of COPD is tobacco smoke. However, less than 50 percent '
             'of heavy smokers develop COPD, so other factors need to be considered, including '
             'exposure to indoor and outdoor pollutants, allergens, occupational exposure, and '
             'host factors. One of the known causes of COPD is exposure to construction dust. The '
             'three main types of construction dust are silica dust, non-silica dust (e.g., dust '
             'from gypsum, cement, limestone, marble and dolomite) and wood dust. Host factors '
             'include a genetic susceptibility, <a href="Socioeconomic_status#Health" '
             'title="Socioeconomic status">factors associated with poverty</a>, aging and physical '
             'inactivity. <a href="Asthma" title="Asthma">Asthma</a> and <a href="Tuberculosis" '
             'title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a> are also recognized as risk factors, as the '
             'comorbidity of COPD is reported to be 12 times higher in patients with asthma after '
             'adjusting for smoking history. In Europe, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Airway_hyperresponsiveness" title="Airway hyperresponsiveness">airway '
             'hyperresponsiveness</a> is rated as the second most important risk factor after '
             'smoking. </p><p>A host factor of an <a href="Lung#Variation" title="Lung">airway '
             'branching variation</a>, arising during <a href="Lung#Development" '
             'title="Lung">development</a>, has been described. The <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Respiratory_tree" title="Respiratory tree">respiratory tree</a> is a filter '
             'for harmful substances, and any variant has the potential to disrupt this. A '
             'variation is associated with the development of chronic bronchitis, and another with '
             'the development of emphysema. A branch variant in the central airway is specifically '
             'associated with an increased susceptibility for the later development of COPD. A '
             'genetic association for the variants has been sometimes found with <i>FGF10</i>. '
             '</p><p><a href="Alcohol_abuse" title="Alcohol abuse">Alcohol abuse</a> can lead to '
             '<a href="Alcoholic_lung_disease" title="Alcoholic lung disease">alcoholic lung '
             'disease</a> and is seen to be an independent risk factor for COPD. <a '
             'href="Mucociliary_clearance" title="Mucociliary clearance">Mucociliary clearance</a> '
             'is disrupted by chronic exposure to alcohol; <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Pulmonary_macrophage" title="Pulmonary macrophage">macrophage</a> activity is '
             'diminished and an inflammatory response promoted. The damage leads to a '
             'susceptibility for infection, including <a href="COVID-19" '
             'title="COVID-19">COVID-19</a>, more so when combined with smoking; smoking induces '
             'the upregulation of the expression of <a class="mw-redirect" href="ACE2" '
             'title="ACE2">ACE2</a>, a receptor for the <a href="SARS-CoV-2" '
             'title="SARS-CoV-2">SARS-CoV-2</a> virus. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Smoking">Smoking</h3></div> <p>The primary risk factor for COPD '
             'globally is <a href="Tobacco_smoking" title="Tobacco smoking">tobacco smoking</a>, '
             'with an increased rate of developing COPD shown in smokers and ex-smokers. Of those '
             'who smoke, about 20% will get COPD, increasing to less than 50% in heavy smokers. In '
             'the United States and United Kingdom, of those with COPD, 80–95% are either current '
             'or previous smokers. Several studies indicate that women are more susceptible than '
             'men to the harmful effects of tobacco smoke. For the same amount of cigarette '
             'smoking, women have a higher risk of COPD than men. Women who smoke during <a '
             'href="Pregnancy" title="Pregnancy">pregnancy</a>, and during the early life of the '
             'child is a risk factor for the later development of COPD in their child. Epigenetic '
             'studies support this link, showing that <i>ACSF3</i> is differentially methylated in '
             'smoke-exposed <a href="Fetus" title="Fetus">fetal</a> lungs, and an integrative '
             'study identified it as a key regulator of COPD. </p><p>Inhaled smoke triggers the '
             'release of excessive proteases in lungs, which then degrades elastin, the major '
             'component of alveoli. Smoke also impairs the action of <a href="Cilium" '
             'title="Cilium">cilia</a>, inhibiting <a href="Mucociliary_clearance" '
             'title="Mucociliary clearance">mucociliary clearance</a> that clears the bronchi of '
             'mucus, cellular debris and unwanted fluid. </p><p>Other types of tobacco smoke, such '
             'as from cigar, pipe, water-pipe and <a href="Hookah" title="Hookah">hookah</a> use, '
             'also confer a risk. Water-pipe or hookah smoke appears to be as harmful or even more '
             'harmful than smoking cigarettes. </p><p><a class="mw-redirect" href="Marijuana" '
             'title="Marijuana">Marijuana</a> is the second most commonly smoked substance, but '
             'evidence linking its use to COPD is very limited. Limited evidence shows that '
             'marijuana does not accelerate lung function decline. A low use of marijuana gives a '
             'bronchodilatory effect rather than the bronchoconstrictive effect from tobacco use, '
             'but it is often smoked in combination with tobacco or on its own by tobacco smokers. '
             'Higher use however has shown a decline in the <a class="mw-redirect" href="FEV1" '
             'title="FEV1">FEV1</a>. There is evidence of it causing some respiratory problems and '
             'its use in combination may have a cumulative toxic effect, suggesting it as a risk '
             'factor for spontaneous pneumothorax, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Bullous_emphysema" '
             'title="Bullous emphysema">bullous emphysema</a>, COPD and lung cancer. A noted '
             'difference between marijuana use and tobacco was that respiratory problems were '
             'resolved with stopping usage unlike the continued decline with stopping tobacco '
             'smoking. Respiratory symptoms reported with marijuana use included chronic cough, '
             'increased sputum production and wheezing, but not shortness of breath. Also, these '
             'symptoms were typically reported ten years ahead of their onset in tobacco smokers. '
             'Another study found that chronic marijuana smokers even with the additional use of '
             'tobacco developed similar respiratory problems, but did not seem to develop airflow '
             'limitation and COPD. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Pollution">Pollution</h3></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" '
             'role="note">See also: <a class="mw-redirect" href="Health_effects_of_wood_smoke" '
             'title="Health effects of wood smoke">Health effects of wood smoke</a></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="600" data-file-width="850" decoding="async" height="247" '
             'loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Access_to_clean_fuels_and_technologies_for_cooking%252C_OWID.svg.png" '
             'width="350"/><figcaption>Access to clean fuel and clean cooking facilities as of '
             '2016</figcaption></figure> <p>Exposure to particulates can bring about the '
             'development of COPD or its exacerbations. Those with COPD are more susceptible to '
             'the harmful effects of particulate exposure that can cause acute exacerbations '
             'brought about by infections. Black carbon also known as <i>soot</i>, is an <a '
             'href="Air_pollution" title="Air pollution">air pollutant</a> associated with an '
             'increased risk of hospitalization due to the exacerbations caused. Long-term '
             'exposure is indicated as an increased rate of mortality in COPD. Studies have shown '
             'that people who live in large cities have a higher rate of COPD compared to people '
             'who live in rural areas. Areas with poor outdoor air quality, including that from '
             'exhaust gas, generally have higher rates of COPD. Urban air pollution significantly '
             'affects the developing lung and its maturation, and contributes a potential risk '
             'factor for the later development of COPD. The overall effect in relation to smoking '
             'is believed to be small. </p><p>Poorly ventilated fires used for cooking and '
             'heating, are often fueled by coal or biomass such as wood and <a '
             'href="Dry_dung_fuel" title="Dry dung fuel">dry dung</a>, leading to indoor air '
             'pollution and are one of the most common causes of COPD in <a '
             'href="Developing_country" title="Developing country">developing countries</a>. Women '
             'are affected more as they have a greater exposure. These fuels are used as the main '
             'source of energy in 80% of homes in India, China and sub-Saharan Africa. </p><p> '
             '</p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Occupational_exposure">Occupational '
             'exposure</h3></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">See '
             'also: <a href="Cadmium_poisoning" title="Cadmium poisoning">Cadmium '
             'poisoning</a></div> <p>Intense and prolonged exposure to workplace dusts, chemicals '
             'and fumes increases the risk of COPD in smokers, nonsmokers and never-smokers. '
             'Substances implicated in occupational exposure and listed in the UK, include organic '
             'and inorganic dusts such as cadmium, silica, dust from grains and flour and fumes '
             'from cadmium and welding that promote respiratory symptoms. Workplace exposure is '
             'believed to be the cause in 10–20% of cases and in the United States, it is believed '
             'to be related to around 30% of cases among never smokers and probably represents a '
             'greater risk in countries without sufficient regulations. The negative effects of '
             'dust exposure and cigarette smoke exposure appear to be cumulative. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Genetics">Genetics</h3></div> <p>Genetics '
             'plays a role in the development of COPD. It is more common among relatives of those '
             'with COPD who smoke than among unrelated smokers. The most well known genetic risk '
             'factor is <a href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin_deficiency" title="Alpha-1 antitrypsin '
             'deficiency">alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency</a> (AATD) and this is the only genotype '
             '(genetic subtype) with a specific treatment. This risk is particularly high if '
             'someone deficient in <a href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin" title="Alpha-1 '
             'antitrypsin">alpha-1 antitrypsin</a> (AAT) also smokes. It is responsible for about '
             '1–5% of cases and the condition is present in about three to four in 10,000 people. '
             '</p><p>Mutations in <i>MMP1</i> gene that encodes for interstitial collagenase are '
             'associated with COPD. </p><p>The COPDGene study is an ongoing longitudinal study '
             'into the epidemiology of COPD, identifying phenotypes and looking for their likely '
             'association with susceptible genes. <a href="Genome-wide_association_study" '
             'title="Genome-wide association study"> Genome-wide analyses</a> in concert with the '
             '<i>International COPD Genetics Consortium</i> has identified more than 80 genome '
             'regions associated with COPD, and further studies in these regions have been called '
             'for. <a href="Whole_genome_sequencing" title="Whole genome sequencing">Whole genome '
             'sequencing</a> is an ongoing collaboration (2019) with the <a '
             'href="National_Heart%2C_Lung%2C_and_Blood_Institute" title="National Heart, Lung, '
             'and Blood Institute">National Heart, Lung and Blood Institute</a> (NHLBI) to '
             'identify rare genetic determinants. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Pathophysiology">Pathophysiology</h2></div> <figure class="mw-default-size" '
             'typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="653" '
             'data-file-width="475" decoding="async" height="344" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Copd_2010.jpg" width="250"/><figcaption>Normal <a href="Lung" '
             'title="Lung">lungs</a> shown in upper diagram. Lungs damaged by COPD in the lower '
             'diagram with an inset showing a cross-section of <a href="Bronchiole" '
             'title="Bronchiole">bronchioles</a> blocked by <a href="Mucus" '
             'title="Mucus">mucus</a> and damaged <a href="Pulmonary_alveolus" title="Pulmonary '
             'alveolus">alveoli</a>.</figcaption></figure> <p>COPD is a progressive <a '
             'href="Obstructive_lung_disease" title="Obstructive lung disease">lung disease</a> in '
             'which chronic, incompletely reversible poor airflow (airflow limitation) and an '
             'inability to breathe out fully (<a href="Air_trapping" title="Air trapping">air '
             'trapping</a>) exist. The poor airflow is the result of small airways disease and '
             'emphysema (the breakdown of lung tissue). The relative contributions of these two '
             'factors vary between people. Air trapping precedes lung hyperinflation. </p><p>COPD '
             'develops as a significant and chronic inflammatory response to inhaled irritants '
             'which ultimately leads to bronchial and alveolar remodelling in the lung known as '
             '<b>small airways disease</b>. Thus, airway remodelling with narrowing of peripheral '
             'airway and emphysema are responsible for the alteration of lung function. <a '
             'href="Mucociliary_clearance" title="Mucociliary clearance">Mucociliary clearance</a> '
             'is particularly altered with a dysregulation of <a class="mw-redirect" href="Cilia" '
             'title="Cilia">cilia</a> and <a href="Mucus" title="Mucus">mucus</a> production. '
             'Small airway disease sometimes called <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Chronic_bronchiolitis" title="Chronic bronchiolitis">chronic '
             'bronchiolitis</a>, appears to be the precursor for the development of emphysema. The '
             'inflammatory cells involved include <a href="Neutrophil" '
             'title="Neutrophil">neutrophils</a> and <a href="Macrophage" '
             'title="Macrophage">macrophages</a>, two types of white blood cells. Those who smoke '
             'additionally have <a href="Cytotoxic_T_cell" title="Cytotoxic T cell">cytotoxic T '
             'cell</a> involvement, and some people with COPD have <a href="Eosinophil" '
             'title="Eosinophil">eosinophil</a> involvement similar to that in asthma. Part of '
             'this cell response is brought on by inflammatory mediators such as chemotactic '
             'factors. Other processes involved with lung damage include oxidative stress produced '
             'by high concentrations of free radicals in tobacco smoke and released by '
             'inflammatory cells and breakdown of the <a href="Connective_tissue" '
             'title="Connective tissue">connective tissue</a> of the lungs by proteases '
             '(particularly elastase) that are insufficiently inhibited by protease inhibitors. '
             'The destruction of the connective tissue of the lungs leads to emphysema, which then '
             'contributes to the poor airflow and finally, poor absorption and release of '
             'respiratory gases. General muscle wasting that often occurs in COPD may be partly '
             'due to inflammatory mediators released by the lungs into the blood. </p> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="1979" data-file-width="3288" '
             'decoding="async" height="150" loading="lazy" src="./Emphysema_low_mag.jpg" '
             'width="250"/><figcaption><a href="Micrograph" title="Micrograph">Micrograph</a> '
             'showing <a href="Emphysema" title="Emphysema">emphysema</a> (left – large empty '
             'spaces) and lung tissue with relative preserved <a href="Pulmonary_alveolus" '
             'title="Pulmonary alveolus">alveoli</a> (right)</figcaption></figure> <p>Narrowing of '
             'the airways occurs due to inflammation and subsequent scarring within them. This '
             'contributes to the inability to breathe out fully. The greatest reduction in air '
             'flow occurs when breathing out, as the pressure in the chest is compressing the '
             'airways at this time. This can result in more air from the previous breath remaining '
             'within the lungs when the next breath is started, resulting in an increase in the '
             'total volume of air in the lungs at any given time, a process called air trapping '
             'which is closely followed by <a href="Inhalation#Hyperinflation" '
             'title="Inhalation">hyperinflation</a>. Hyperinflation from exercise is linked to '
             'shortness of breath in COPD, as breathing in is less comfortable when the lungs are '
             'already partly filled. Hyperinflation may also worsen during an exacerbation. There '
             'may also be a degree of <a href="Bronchial_hyperresponsiveness" title="Bronchial '
             'hyperresponsiveness">airway hyperresponsiveness</a> to irritants similar to those '
             'found in asthma. </p><p><a href="Hypoxemia" title="Hypoxemia">Low oxygen levels</a> '
             'and eventually, <a href="Hypercapnia" title="Hypercapnia">high carbon dioxide levels '
             'in the blood</a>, can occur from poor <a href="Gas_exchange" title="Gas '
             'exchange">gas exchange</a> due to decreased ventilation from airway obstruction, '
             'hyperinflation and a reduced desire to breathe. During exacerbations, airway '
             'inflammation is also increased, resulting in increased hyperinflation, reduced '
             'expiratory airflow and worsening of gas transfer. This can lead to low blood oxygen '
             'levels, which, if present for a prolonged period, can result in <a '
             'href="Vasoconstriction" title="Vasoconstriction">narrowing of the arteries</a> in '
             'the lungs, while emphysema leads to the breakdown of capillaries in the lungs. Both '
             'of these conditions may result in <a href="Pulmonary_heart_disease" title="Pulmonary '
             'heart disease">pulmonary heart disease</a> also classically known as <i>cor '
             'pulmonale</i>. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Diagnosis">Diagnosis</h2></div> <figure class="mw-default-size" '
             'typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img alt="A person sitting and blowing into a device attached '
             'to a computer" class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="1092" '
             'data-file-width="1508" decoding="async" height="253" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./DoingSpirometry.JPG" width="350"/><figcaption>A person blowing into a <a '
             'href="Spirometer" title="Spirometer">spirometer</a>. Smaller handheld devices are '
             'available for office use.</figcaption></figure> <p>The diagnosis of COPD should be '
             'considered in anyone over the age of 35 to 40 who has <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Dyspnea" title="Dyspnea">shortness of breath</a>, a chronic cough, sputum '
             'production, or frequent winter colds and a history of exposure to risk factors for '
             'the disease. <a href="Spirometry" title="Spirometry">Spirometry</a> is then used to '
             'confirm the diagnosis. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Spirometry">Spirometry</h3></div> <p><a href="Spirometry" '
             'title="Spirometry">Spirometry</a> measures the amount of airflow obstruction present '
             'and is generally carried out after the use of a <a href="Bronchodilator" '
             'title="Bronchodilator">bronchodilator</a>, a medication to open up the airways. Two '
             'main components are measured to make the diagnosis, the <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Forced_expiratory_volume_in_one_second" title="Forced expiratory volume in one '
             'second">forced expiratory volume in one second</a> (FEV1), which is the greatest '
             'volume of air that can be breathed out in the first second of a breath and the <a '
             'href="Vital_capacity" title="Vital capacity">forced vital capacity</a> (FVC), which '
             'is the greatest volume of air that can be breathed out in a single large breath. '
             'Normally, 75–80% of the FVC comes out in the first second and a <a '
             'href="FEV1/FVC_ratio" title="FEV1/FVC ratio">FEV1/FVC ratio</a> less than 70% in '
             'someone with symptoms of COPD defines a person as having the disease. Based on these '
             'measurements, spirometry would lead to over-diagnosis of COPD in the elderly. The <a '
             'href="National_Institute_for_Health_and_Care_Excellence" title="National Institute '
             'for Health and Care Excellence">National Institute for Health and Care '
             'Excellence</a> criteria additionally require a FEV1 less than 80% of predicted. '
             'People with COPD also exhibit a decrease in <a class="mw-redirect" href="DLCO" '
             'title="DLCO">diffusing capacity of the lung for carbon monoxide</a> due to decreased '
             'surface area in the alveoli, as well as damage to the capillary bed. Testing the <a '
             'href="Peak_expiratory_flow" title="Peak expiratory flow">peak expiratory flow</a> '
             '(the maximum speed of expiration), commonly used in asthma diagnosis, is not '
             'sufficient for the diagnosis of COPD. </p><p><a href="Screening_(medicine)" '
             'title="Screening (medicine)">Screening</a> using spirometry in those without '
             'symptoms has uncertain effects and is generally not recommended; however, it is '
             'recommended for those without symptoms but with a known risk factor. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Assessment">Assessment</h3></div> <table '
             'class=\'wikitable"\' style="float: right; margin-left:15px; text-align:center"> '
             '<caption><a href="Medical_Research_Council_(United_Kingdom)" title="Medical Research '
             'Council (United Kingdom)">MRC</a> shortness of breath scale </caption> <tbody><tr> '
             '<th>Grade</th> <th>Activity affected </th></tr> <tr> <td>1</td> <td>Only strenuous '
             'activity </td></tr> <tr> <td>2</td> <td>Vigorous walking </td></tr> <tr> <td>3</td> '
             '<td>With normal walking </td></tr> <tr> <td>4</td> <td>After a few minutes of '
             'walking </td></tr> <tr> <td>5</td> <td>With changing clothing '
             '</td></tr></tbody></table> <table class=\'wikitable"\' style="float: right; '
             'margin-left:15px; text-align:center"> <caption><a '
             'href="Global_Initiative_for_Chronic_Obstructive_Lung_Disease" title="Global '
             'Initiative for Chronic Obstructive Lung Disease">GOLD</a> criteria </caption> '
             '<tbody><tr> <th>Severity</th> <th>FEV1% predicted </th></tr> <tr> <td>Mild (GOLD '
             '1)</td> <td>≥80 </td></tr> <tr> <td>Moderate (GOLD 2)</td> <td>50–79 </td></tr> <tr> '
             '<td>Severe (GOLD 3)</td> <td>30–49 </td></tr> <tr> <td>Very severe (GOLD 4)</td> '
             '<td>&lt;30 </td></tr></tbody></table> <p>A number of methods can be used to assess '
             'the effects and severity of COPD. The <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="MRC_breathlessness_scale" title="MRC breathlessness scale">MRC breathlessness '
             'scale</a> or the COPD assessment test (CAT) are simple questionnaires that may be '
             'used. <a href="Global_Initiative_for_Chronic_Obstructive_Lung_Disease" title="Global '
             'Initiative for Chronic Obstructive Lung Disease">GOLD</a> refers to a modified MRC '
             'scale that if used, needs to include other tests since it is simply a test of '
             'breathlessness experienced. Scores on CAT range from 0–40 with the higher the score, '
             'the more severe the disease. Spirometry may help to determine the severity of '
             'airflow limitation. This is typically based on the FEV1 expressed as a percentage of '
             'the predicted "normal" for the person\'s age, gender, height and weight. Guidelines '
             'published in 2011 by American and European medical societies recommend partly basing '
             'treatment recommendations on the FEV1. The <a '
             'href="Global_Initiative_for_Chronic_Obstructive_Lung_Disease" title="Global '
             'Initiative for Chronic Obstructive Lung Disease">GOLD</a> guidelines group people '
             'into four categories based on symptoms assessment, degree of airflow limitation and '
             'history of exacerbations. Weight loss, muscle loss and fatigue are seen in severe '
             'and very severe cases. </p><p>Use of screening questionnaires, such as the COPD '
             'diagnostic questionnaire (CDQ), alone or in combination with hand-held flow meters, '
             'is appropriate for screening of COPD in primary care. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Other_tests">Other tests</h3></div> <p>A <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Chest_X-ray" title="Chest X-ray">chest X-ray</a> is not useful to establish a '
             'diagnosis of COPD, but it is of use in either excluding other conditions or '
             'including comorbidities such as <a href="Pulmonary_fibrosis" title="Pulmonary '
             'fibrosis">pulmonary fibrosis</a> and <a href="Bronchiectasis" '
             'title="Bronchiectasis">bronchiectasis</a>. Characteristic signs of COPD on X-ray '
             'include hyperinflation (shown by a flattened diaphragm and an increased retrosternal '
             'air space) and lung hyperlucency. A <a href="Saber-sheath_trachea" '
             'title="Saber-sheath trachea">saber-sheath trachea</a> may also be shown that is <a '
             'href="Pathognomonic" title="Pathognomonic">indicative</a> of COPD. </p><p>A <a '
             'href="CT_scan" title="CT scan">CT scan</a> is not routinely used except for the '
             'exclusion of bronchiectasis. <a href="Pulse_oximetry" title="Pulse oximetry">Pulse '
             'oximetry</a> measurement of peripheral oxygen saturation is recommended in people '
             'with clinical signs of respiratory failure or right heart failure. An <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Arterial_blood_gas" title="Arterial blood gas">analysis of '
             'arterial blood</a> is recommended in those with a peripheral oxygen saturation of '
             '92% or less to determine actual blood oxygen level and assess for <a '
             'href="Hypercapnia" title="Hypercapnia">high levels of carbon dioxide</a> in the '
             'blood, which may have therapeutic implications such as need for <a '
             'href="Non-invasive_ventilation" title="Non-invasive ventilation">non-invasive '
             'ventilation</a> or oxygen supplementation. <a href="World_Health_Organization" '
             'title="World Health Organization">WHO</a> recommends that all those diagnosed with '
             'COPD be screened for <a href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin_deficiency" title="Alpha-1 '
             'antitrypsin deficiency">alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency</a>. </p> <ul class="gallery '
             'mw-gallery-traditional"> <li class="gallerybox" style="width: 155px"> <div '
             'class="thumb" style="width: 150px; height: 150px;"><span typeof="mw:File"><img '
             'alt="A black and white image, with a small white heart in the middle and large black '
             'lungs around it" class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="4019" '
             'data-file-width="2998" decoding="async" height="120" loading="lazy" src="./COPD.JPG" '
             'width="89"/></span></div> <div class="gallerytext">Chest X-ray demonstrating severe '
             'COPD, displaying small heart size in comparison to the lungs</div> </li> <li '
             'class="gallerybox" style="width: 155px"> <div class="thumb" style="width: 150px; '
             'height: 150px;"><span typeof="mw:File"><img alt="A lateral chest X-ray of a person '
             'with emphysema, displaying barrel chest and flat diaphragm" class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="2686" data-file-width="2270" decoding="async" height="120" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Barrowchest.JPG" width="101"/></span></div> <div '
             'class="gallerytext">A lateral chest X-ray of a person with emphysema, displaying <a '
             'href="Barrel_chest" title="Barrel chest">barrel chest</a> and flat diaphragm</div> '
             '</li> <li class="gallerybox" style="width: 155px"> <div class="thumb" style="width: '
             '150px; height: 150px;"><span typeof="mw:File"><img alt="Lung bulla as seen on chest '
             'X-ray in a person with severe COPD" class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="959" '
             'data-file-width="1010" decoding="async" height="114" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./BullaCXR.PNG" width="120"/></span></div> <div class="gallerytext">Lung bulla '
             'as seen on chest X-ray in a person with severe COPD</div> </li> <li '
             'class="gallerybox" style="width: 155px"> <div class="thumb" style="width: 150px; '
             'height: 150px;"><span typeof="mw:File"><img alt="A severe case of bullous emphysema" '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="2040" data-file-width="2040" '
             'decoding="async" height="120" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Medical_X-Ray_imaging_WFH07_nevit.jpg" width="120"/></span></div> <div '
             'class="gallerytext">A severe case of bullous emphysema</div> </li> <li '
             'class="gallerybox" style="width: 155px"> <div class="thumb" style="width: 150px; '
             'height: 150px;"><span typeof="mw:File"><img alt="Axial CT image of the lung of a '
             'person with end-stage bullous emphysema" class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="772" data-file-width="1019" decoding="async" height="91" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Bullus_emphasemaCT.png" width="120"/></span></div> <div '
             'class="gallerytext">Axial CT image of the lung of a person with end-stage bullous '
             'emphysema</div> </li> <li class="gallerybox" style="width: 155px"> <div '
             'class="thumb" style="width: 150px; height: 150px;"><span typeof="mw:File"><img '
             'alt="Very severe emphysema with lung cancer on the left (CT scan)" '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="810" data-file-width="810" '
             'decoding="async" height="120" loading="lazy" src="./EmphysemaPlusLungCA.png" '
             'width="120"/></span></div> <div class="gallerytext">Very severe emphysema with lung '
             'cancer on the left (CT scan)</div> </li> </ul> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Differential_diagnosis">Differential diagnosis</h3></div> '
             '<p>COPD may need to be <a href="Differential_diagnosis" title="Differential '
             'diagnosis">differentiated</a> from other conditions such as <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Congestive_heart_failure" title="Congestive heart failure">congestive heart '
             'failure</a>, <a href="Asthma" title="Asthma">asthma</a>, <a href="Bronchiectasis" '
             'title="Bronchiectasis">bronchiectasis</a>, <a href="Tuberculosis" '
             'title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Obliterative_bronchiolitis" title="Obliterative bronchiolitis">obliterative '
             'bronchiolitis</a>, <a href="Lung_cancer" title="Lung cancer">lung cancer</a>, and <a '
             'href="Diffuse_panbronchiolitis" title="Diffuse panbronchiolitis">diffuse '
             'panbronchiolitis</a>. The distinction between asthma and COPD is made based on the '
             'symptoms, smoking history and whether airflow limitation is reversible with '
             'bronchodilators at spirometry. Chronic bronchitis with normal airflow is not '
             'classified as COPD. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Prevention">Prevention</h2></div> <p>Most cases of COPD are potentially '
             'preventable through decreasing exposure to tobacco smoke and other indoor and '
             'outdoor pollutants. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Smoking_cessation">Smoking cessation</h3></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">See also: <a href="World_No_Tobacco_Day" '
             'title="World No Tobacco Day">World No Tobacco Day</a></div> <p>The <a '
             'href="Tobacco_smoking#Legal_issues_and_regulation" title="Tobacco '
             'smoking">policies</a> of governments, public health agencies and antismoking '
             'organizations can reduce smoking rates by discouraging people from starting and '
             'encouraging people to stop smoking. <a href="Smoking_ban" title="Smoking '
             'ban">Smoking bans</a> in public areas and places of work are important measures to '
             'decrease exposure to secondhand smoke, and while many places have instituted bans, '
             'more are recommended. </p><p>In those who smoke, <a href="Smoking_cessation" '
             'title="Smoking cessation">stopping smoking</a> is the only measure shown to slow '
             'down the worsening of COPD. Even at a late stage of the disease, it can reduce the '
             'rate of worsening lung function and delay the onset of disability and death. Often, '
             'several attempts are required before long-term abstinence is achieved. Attempts over '
             '5 years lead to success in nearly 40% of people. </p><p>Some smokers can achieve '
             'long-term smoking cessation through willpower alone. Smoking, however, is highly '
             'addictive, and many smokers need further support. The chance of quitting is improved '
             'with social support, engagement in a smoking cessation program and the use of '
             'medications such as <a href="Nicotine_replacement_therapy" title="Nicotine '
             'replacement therapy">nicotine replacement therapy</a>, <a href="Bupropion" '
             'title="Bupropion">bupropion</a>, or <a href="Varenicline" '
             'title="Varenicline">varenicline</a>. Combining smoking-cessation medication with '
             'behavioral therapy is more than twice as likely to be effective in helping people '
             'with COPD stop smoking, compared with behavioral therapy alone. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Occupational_health">Occupational '
             'health</h3></div> <p>Several measures have been taken to reduce the likelihood that '
             'workers in at-risk industries—such as coal mining, construction and '
             'stonemasonry—will develop COPD. Examples of these measures include the creation of '
             'public policy, education of workers and management about the risks, promoting '
             'smoking cessation, <a href="Disease_surveillance" title="Disease '
             'surveillance">checking</a> workers for early signs of COPD, use of <a '
             'href="Respirator" title="Respirator">respirators</a> and dust control. Effective '
             'dust control can be achieved by improving ventilation, using water sprays and by '
             'using mining techniques that minimize dust generation. If a worker develops COPD, '
             'further lung damage can be reduced by avoiding ongoing dust exposure, for example by '
             'changing their work role. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Pollution_control">Pollution control</h3></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Further information: Indoor air pollution in '
             'developing nations and <a href="Air_pollution#Pollution_control" title="Air '
             'pollution">Air pollution §\xa0Pollution control</a></div> <p>Both indoor and outdoor '
             'air quality can be improved, which may prevent COPD or slow the worsening of '
             'existing disease. This may be achieved by public policy efforts, cultural changes '
             'and personal involvement. Many developed countries have successfully improved '
             'outdoor air quality through regulations, which has resulted in improvements in the '
             'lung function of their populations. Individuals are also advised to avoid irritants '
             'of indoor and outdoor pollution. </p><p>In developing countries, one key effort is '
             'to reduce exposure to smoke from cooking and heating fuels through improved '
             'ventilation of homes and better stoves and chimneys. Proper stoves may improve '
             'indoor air quality by 85%. Using alternative energy sources such as solar cooking '
             'and electrical heating is also effective. Using fuels such as kerosene or coal might '
             'produce less household particulate matter than traditional biomass such as wood or '
             'dung, but whether this is better health wise is unclear. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Management">Management</h2></div> <p>COPD currently has no '
             'cure, but the symptoms are treatable and its progression can be delayed, '
             'particularly by <a href="Smoking_cessation" title="Smoking cessation">stopping '
             'smoking</a>. The major goals of management are to reduce exposure to risk factors '
             'including offering non-pharmacological treatments such as help with stopping '
             'smoking. Stopping smoking can reduce the rate of lung function decline and also '
             'reduce mortality from smoking-related diseases such as lung cancer and '
             'cardiovascular disease. Other recommendations include <a href="Pneumococcal_vaccine" '
             'title="Pneumococcal vaccine">pneumococcal vaccination</a> and yearly <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Influenza_vaccination" title="Influenza '
             'vaccination">influenza vaccination</a> to help reduce the risk of exacerbations; as '
             'of 2024 CDC and GOLD also recommend <a class="mw-redirect" href="RSV_vaccine" '
             'title="RSV vaccine">RSV vaccine</a> for individuals above 60 years. Guidance is also '
             'advised as to managing breathlessness and stress. </p><p><a href="Comorbidity" '
             'title="Comorbidity">Other illnesses</a> are also being managed. An action plan is '
             'drawn up and is to be reviewed. Providing people with a personalized action plan, an '
             'educational session and support for use of their action plan in the event of an '
             'exacerbation, reduces the number of hospital visits and encourages early treatment '
             'of exacerbations. When self-management interventions, such as taking corticosteroids '
             'and using supplemental oxygen, are combined with action plans, health-related '
             'quality of life is improved compared to usual care. In those with COPD who are <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Malnourished" title="Malnourished">malnourished</a>, '
             'supplementation with <a href="Vitamin_C" title="Vitamin C">vitamin C</a>, <a '
             'href="Vitamin_E" title="Vitamin E">vitamin E</a>, <a href="Zinc" '
             'title="Zinc">zinc</a> and selenium can improve weight, strength of <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Respiratory_muscles" title="Respiratory '
             'muscles">respiratory muscles</a> and health-related quality of life. Significant <a '
             'href="Vitamin_D_deficiency" title="Vitamin D deficiency">vitamin D deficiency</a> is '
             'common in those with COPD and can cause increased exacerbations. Supplementation '
             'when deficient can give a 50% reduction in the number of exacerbations. '
             '</p><p>Several <a href="Pharmacotherapy" title="Pharmacotherapy">medical '
             'treatments</a> are used in the management of stable COPD and exacerbations. These '
             'include <a href="Bronchodilator" title="Bronchodilator">bronchodilators</a>, <a '
             'href="Corticosteroid" title="Corticosteroid">corticosteroids</a> and <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Antibiotics" title="Antibiotics">antibiotics</a>. '
             '</p><p>In those with a severe exacerbation, antibiotics improve outcomes. Several '
             'different antibiotics may be used, including <a href="Amoxicillin" '
             'title="Amoxicillin">amoxicillin</a>, <a href="Doxycycline" '
             'title="Doxycycline">doxycycline</a> and <a href="Azithromycin" '
             'title="Azithromycin">azithromycin</a>; whether one is better than the others is '
             'unclear. There is no clear evidence of improved outcomes for those with less severe '
             'cases. The FDA recommends against the use of <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Fluoroquinolones" title="Fluoroquinolones">fluoroquinolones</a> when other '
             'options are available due to higher risks of serious side effects. In treating <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Acute_hypercapnic_respiratory_failure" title="Acute '
             'hypercapnic respiratory failure">acute hypercapnic respiratory failure</a> (acutely '
             'raised levels of carbon dioxide), <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Bilevel_positive_airway_pressure" title="Bilevel positive airway '
             'pressure">bilevel positive airway pressure</a> (BPAP) can decrease mortality and the '
             'need of <a href="Intensive_care_medicine" title="Intensive care medicine">intensive '
             'care</a>. </p><p>In those with <a href="End_stage_pulmonary_disease" title="End '
             'stage pulmonary disease">end-stage disease</a>, <a href="Palliative_care" '
             'title="Palliative care">palliative care</a> is focused on relieving symptoms. <a '
             'href="Morphine" title="Morphine">Morphine</a> can improve exercise tolerance. <a '
             'href="Non-invasive_ventilation" title="Non-invasive ventilation">Non-invasive '
             'ventilation</a> may be used to support breathing and also reduce daytime '
             'breathlessness. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Bronchodilators">Bronchodilators</h3></div> <p>Inhaled short-acting <a '
             'href="Bronchodilator" title="Bronchodilator">bronchodilators</a> are the primary '
             'medications used on an <a href="Pro_re_nata" title="Pro re nata">as needed</a> '
             'basis; their use regularly is not recommended. The two major types are <a '
             'href="Beta2-adrenergic_agonist" title="Beta2-adrenergic agonist">beta2-adrenergic '
             'agonists</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Anticholinergics" '
             'title="Anticholinergics">anticholinergics</a>; either in long-acting or short-acting '
             'forms. Beta2–adrenergic agonists target receptors in the <a href="Smooth_muscle" '
             'title="Smooth muscle">smooth muscle cells</a> in <a href="Bronchiole" '
             'title="Bronchiole">bronchioles</a> causing them to relax and allow improved airflow. '
             'They reduce shortness of breath, tend to reduce <a href="Dynamic_hyperinflation" '
             'title="Dynamic hyperinflation">dynamic hyperinflation</a> and improve exercise '
             'tolerance. Short-acting bronchodilators have an effect for four hours and for '
             'maintenance therapy long acting bronchodilators with an effect of over twelve hours '
             'are used. In times of more severe symptoms, a short-acting agent may be used in '
             'combination. An inhaled corticosteroid used with a long-acting beta-2 agonist is '
             'more effective than either one on its own. </p><p>Which type of long-acting agent, '
             '<a class="mw-redirect" href="Long-acting_muscarinic_antagonist" title="Long-acting '
             'muscarinic antagonist">long-acting muscarinic antagonist</a> (LAMA) such as <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Tiotropium" title="Tiotropium">tiotropium</a> or <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Long-acting_beta_agonist" title="Long-acting beta '
             'agonist">long-acting beta agonist</a> (LABA), is better is unclear, and trying each '
             'and continuing with the one that works best may be advisable. Both types of agent '
             'appear to reduce the risk of acute exacerbations by 15–25%. The combination of '
             'LABA/LAMA may reduce COPD exacerbations and improve quality-of-life compared to '
             'long-acting bronchodilators alone. The 2018 NICE guideline recommends use of dual '
             'long-acting bronchodilators with economic modelling suggesting that this approach is '
             'preferable to starting one long-acting bronchodilator and adding another later. '
             '</p><p>Several short-acting β agonists are available, including <a href="Salbutamol" '
             'title="Salbutamol">salbutamol</a> (albuterol) and <a href="Terbutaline" '
             'title="Terbutaline">terbutaline</a>. They provide relief of symptoms for four to six '
             'hours. A <a class="mw-redirect" href="Long-acting_beta_agonist" title="Long-acting '
             'beta agonist">long-acting beta agonist</a> (LABA) such as <a href="Salmeterol" '
             'title="Salmeterol">salmeterol</a>, <a href="Formoterol" '
             'title="Formoterol">formoterol</a> and <a href="Indacaterol" '
             'title="Indacaterol">indacaterol</a> are often used as maintenance therapy, with a '
             'duration of action of 12 to 24 hours. Some feel the evidence of benefits is limited, '
             'while others view the evidence of benefit as established. Long-term use of LABAs '
             'appears safe in COPD, with adverse effects include <a href="Tremor" '
             'title="Tremor">shakiness</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Palpitation" '
             'title="Palpitation">heart palpitations</a>. When used with inhaled steroids they '
             'increase the risk of pneumonia. While steroids and LABAs may work better together, '
             'it is unclear if this slight benefit outweighs the increased risks. There is some '
             'evidence that combined treatment of LABAs with long-acting <a '
             'href="Muscarinic_antagonist" title="Muscarinic antagonist">muscarinic '
             'antagonists</a> (LAMA), an anticholinergic, and LABA +ICS (inhaled corticosteroid) '
             "may be similar in benefits in terms of fewer exacerbation's and quality of life "
             'measures for moderate to severe COPD, but LAMA+LABA offers better improvements in '
             'forced expiratory volume (<a class="mw-redirect" href="FEV1%25" '
             'title="FEV1%">FEV1%</a>) and a lower risk of pneumonia. All three together, LABA, '
             'LAMA and ICS, have some evidence of benefits. Indacaterol requires an inhaled dose '
             'once a day and is as effective as the other long-acting β agonist drugs that require '
             'twice-daily dosing for people with stable COPD. </p><p>The two main anticholinergics '
             'used in COPD are <a class="mw-redirect" href="Ipratropium" '
             'title="Ipratropium">ipratropium</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Tiotropium" '
             'title="Tiotropium">tiotropium</a>. Ipratropium is a <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Short-acting_muscarinic_antagonist" title="Short-acting muscarinic '
             'antagonist">short-acting muscarinic antagonist</a> (SAMA), while tiotropium is <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Long-acting_muscarinic_antagonist" title="Long-acting '
             'muscarinic antagonist">long-acting muscarinic antagonist</a> (LAMA). Tiotropium is '
             'associated with a decrease in exacerbations and improved quality of life, and '
             'tiotropium provides those benefits better than ipratropium. Tiotropium does not '
             'appear to affect mortality or the overall hospitalization rate. Anticholinergics can '
             'cause dry mouth and urinary tract symptoms. They are also associated with increased '
             'risk of heart disease and <a href="Stroke" title="Stroke">stroke</a>. <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Aclidinium" title="Aclidinium">Aclidinium</a>, another '
             'long-acting agent, reduces hospitalizations associated with COPD and improves '
             'quality of life. The LAMA <a href="Umeclidinium_bromide" title="Umeclidinium '
             'bromide">umeclidinium bromide</a> is another anticholinergic alternative. When '
             'compared to tiotropium, the LAMAs aclidinium, glycopyrronium, and umeclidinium '
             'appear to have a similar level of efficacy, with all four being more effective than '
             '<a href="Placebo" title="Placebo">placebo</a>. Further research is needed comparing '
             'aclidinium to tiotropium. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Corticosteroids">Corticosteroids</h3></div> <p>Inhaled <a href="Corticosteroid" '
             'title="Corticosteroid">corticosteroids</a> are anti-inflammatories that are '
             'recommended by <a href="Global_Initiative_for_Chronic_Obstructive_Lung_Disease" '
             'title="Global Initiative for Chronic Obstructive Lung Disease">GOLD</a> as a '
             'first-line maintenance treatment in COPD cases with repeated exacerbations. Their '
             'regular use increases the risk of pneumonia in severe cases. Studies have shown that '
             'the risk of pneumonia is associated with all types of corticosteroids; is related to '
             'the disease severity and a dose-response relationship has been noted. Oral <a '
