             'therapy</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Lung_volume_reduction" title="Lung volume '
             'reduction">lung volume reduction</a></td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row"><a href="Medication" title="Medication">Medication</a></th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">Inhaled <a href="Bronchodilator" '
             'title="Bronchodilator">bronchodilators</a> and <a href="Corticosteroid" '
             'title="Corticosteroid">corticosteroids</a></td></tr></tbody></table> '
             "<p><b>Emphysema</b> is any air-filled enlargement in the body's tissues. Most "
             'commonly emphysema refers to the permanent enlargement of air spaces (<a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Alveoli" title="Alveoli">alveoli</a>) in the <a '
             'href="Lung" title="Lung">lungs</a>, and is also known as <b>pulmonary emphysema</b>. '
             '</p><p>Emphysema is a <a class="mw-redirect" href="Lower_respiratory_tract" '
             'title="Lower respiratory tract">lower respiratory tract</a> disease, characterised '
             'by enlarged air-filled spaces in the <a class="mw-redirect" href="Lungs" '
             'title="Lungs">lungs</a>, that can vary in size and may be very large. The spaces are '
             'caused by the breakdown of the <a class="mw-redirect" href="Alveolar_wall" '
             'title="Alveolar wall">walls</a> of the <a href="Pulmonary_alveolus" title="Pulmonary '
             'alveolus">alveoli</a>, which replace the spongy lung tissue. This reduces the total '
             'alveolar surface available for <a href="Gas_exchange" title="Gas exchange">gas '
             'exchange</a> leading to a reduction in oxygen supply for the blood. Emphysema '
             'usually affects the middle aged or older population because it takes time to develop '
             'with the effects of <a href="Tobacco_smoking" title="Tobacco smoking">tobacco '
             'smoking</a> and other risk factors. <a href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin_deficiency" '
             'title="Alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency">Alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency</a> is a '
             'genetic risk factor that may lead to the condition presenting earlier. </p><p>When '
             'associated with significant airflow limitation, emphysema is a <a '
             'href="Chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease#Subtypes" title="Chronic obstructive '
             'pulmonary disease">major subtype</a> of <a '
             'href="Chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Chronic obstructive pulmonary '
             'disease">chronic obstructive pulmonary disease</a> (COPD), a progressive lung '
             'disease characterized by long-term breathing problems and poor airflow. Without '
             'COPD, the finding of emphysema on <a class="mw-redirect" href="Chest_CT" '
             'title="Chest CT">a CT lung scan</a> still confers a higher mortality risk in <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Tobacco_smokers" title="Tobacco smokers">tobacco '
             'smokers</a>. In 2016 in the United States there were 6,977 deaths from emphysema – '
             '2.2 per 100,000 people. Globally it accounts for 5% of all deaths. A 2018 review of '
             'work on the effects of tobacco and cannabis smoking found that a possibly cumulative '
             'toxic effect could be a risk factor for developing emphysema and <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Spontaneous_pneumothorax" title="Spontaneous '
             'pneumothorax">spontaneous pneumothorax</a>. </p><p>There are four types of '
             'emphysema, three of which are related to the anatomy of the <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Pulmonary_lobule" title="Pulmonary lobule">lobules of the lung</a> – '
             'centrilobular or centriacinar, panlobular or panacinar, and paraseptal or distal '
             'acinar emphysema – and are not associated with <a href="Pulmonary_fibrosis" '
             'title="Pulmonary fibrosis">fibrosis</a> (scarring). The fourth type is known as '
             'paracicatricial emphysema or irregular emphysema that involves the <a '
             'href="Lung#Respiratory_zone" title="Lung">acinus</a> irregularly and is associated '
             'with fibrosis. Though the different types can be seen on <a href="Medical_imaging" '
             'title="Medical imaging">imaging</a> they are not well-defined clinically. There are '
             'also a number of associated conditions, including <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Bullous_emphysema" title="Bullous emphysema">bullous emphysema</a>, <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Focal_emphysema" title="Focal emphysema">focal '
             'emphysema</a>, and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Ritalin_lung" title="Ritalin '
             'lung">Ritalin lung</a>. Only the first two types of emphysema – centrilobular and '
             'panlobular – are associated with significant airflow obstruction, with that of '
             'centrilobular emphysema around 20 times more common than panlobular. Centrilobular '
             'emphysema is the only type associated with <a href="Tobacco_smoking" title="Tobacco '
             'smoking">smoking</a>. </p><p><a href="Osteoporosis" '
             'title="Osteoporosis">Osteoporosis</a> is often a <a href="Comorbidity" '
             'title="Comorbidity">comorbidity</a> of emphysema. The use of <a '
             'href="Systemic_administration" title="Systemic administration">systemic</a> <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Corticosteroids" '
             'title="Corticosteroids">corticosteroids</a> for treating exacerbations is a '
             'significant risk factor for osteoporosis, and their repeated use is recommended '
             'against. </p> <meta property="mw:PageProp/toc"/> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Signs_and_symptoms">Signs and symptoms</h2></div> <figure '
             'typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="1500" '
             'data-file-width="1500" decoding="async" height="300" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Blausen_0343_Emphysema.png" width="300"/><figcaption>Diagram of alveoli with '
             'emphysema</figcaption></figure> <p>Emphysema is a respiratory disease of the <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Lower_respiratory_tract" title="Lower respiratory '
             'tract">lower respiratory tract</a>. It is commonly caused by <a '
             'href="Tobacco_smoking" title="Tobacco smoking">tobacco smoking</a> but some people '
             'are affected who have never smoked. The presence of emphysema is a clear risk factor '
             'for lung cancer, made stronger in those who smoke. </p><p>Early symptoms of '
             'emphysema vary. They can include a cough (with or without sputum), wheezing, a fast '
             'breathing rate, breathlessness on exertion, and a feeling of tightness in the chest. '
             'There may be frequent cold or flu infections. Other symptoms may include anxiety, '
             'depression, fatigue, sleep problems and weight loss. These symptoms could also '
             'relate to other lung conditions or other health problems; therefore, emphysema is '
             'often underdiagnosed. The shortness of breath emphysema causes can increase over '
             'time and develop into <a href="Chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Chronic '
             'obstructive pulmonary disease">chronic obstructive pulmonary disease</a>. </p><p>A '
             'sign of emphysema in smokers is a higher number of <a href="Alveolar_macrophage" '
             'title="Alveolar macrophage">alveolar macrophages</a> sampled from the <a '
             'href="Bronchoalveolar_lavage" title="Bronchoalveolar lavage">bronchoalveolar '
             'lavage</a> (BAL) in the lungs. The number can be four to six times greater in those '
             'who smoke than in non-smokers. </p><p>Emphysema is also associated with <a '
             'href="Barrel_chest" title="Barrel chest">barrel chest</a>. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Types">Types</h2></div> <p>There are four '
             'main types of emphysema, three of which are related to the anatomy of the <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Pulmonary_lobule" title="Pulmonary lobule">lobules of the '
             'lung</a> – centrilobular or centriacinar, panlobular or panacinar, and paraseptal or '
             'distal acinar and are not associated with <a href="Pulmonary_fibrosis" '
             'title="Pulmonary fibrosis">fibrosis</a> (scarring). Although fibrosis is not a '
             'normal feature of these subtypes, repair strategies in end-stage emphysema may lead '
             'to <a href="Pulmonary_fibrosis" title="Pulmonary fibrosis">pulmonary fibrosis</a>. '
             'The fourth subtype is known as paracicatricial emphysema or irregular emphysema, '
             'involves the acinus irregularly and is associated with fibrosis. </p><p>Only the '
             'first two types of emphysema – centrilobular and panlobular – are associated with '
             'significant airflow obstruction, with that of centrilobular emphysema around 20 '
             'times more common than panlobular. The subtypes can be seen on <a '
             'href="Medical_imaging" title="Medical imaging">imaging</a> but are not well-defined '
             'clinically. There are also a number of associated conditions including bullous '
             'emphysema, focal emphysema, and Ritalin lung. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Centrilobular">Centrilobular</h3></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="393" data-file-width="528" decoding="async" height="186" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Emphysema_H_and_E.jpg" width="250"/><figcaption>Stained lung '
             'tissue from end-stage emphysema</figcaption></figure> <p>Centrilobular emphysema, '
             'also called <b>centriacinar emphysema</b>, affects the centre of a <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Pulmonary_lobule" title="Pulmonary lobule">pulmonary '
             'lobule</a> (centrilobular) in the lung, the area around the terminal bronchiole and '
             'the first respiratory bronchiole, and can be seen on imaging as an area around the '
             'tip of the visible pulmonary artery. Centrilobular emphysema is the most common type '
             'usually associated with smoking, and with <a href="Bronchitis#Chronic_bronchitis" '
             'title="Bronchitis">chronic bronchitis</a>. The disease progresses from the '
             'centrilobular portion, leaving the lung parenchyma in the surrounding (perilobular) '
             'region preserved. Usually the upper lobes of the lungs are affected. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Panlobular">Panlobular</h3></div> '
             '<p>Panlobular emphysema, also called <b>panacinar emphysema</b>, affects all of the '
             'alveoli in a lobule, and can involve the whole lung or mainly the lower lobes. This '
             'type of emphysema is associated with <a href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin_deficiency" '
             'title="Alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency">alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency</a> (A1AD or '
             'AATD), and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Ritalin_lung" title="Ritalin lung">Ritalin '
             'lung</a>, and is not related to smoking. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading4"><h4 id="Complications">Complications</h4></div> <p>Likely complications '
             'of centrilobular and panlobular emphysema, some of which are life-threatening, '
             'include: <a href="Respiratory_failure" title="Respiratory failure">respiratory '
             'failure</a>, <a href="Pneumonia" title="Pneumonia">pneumonia</a>, <a '
             'href="Acute_exacerbation_of_chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Acute '
             'exacerbation of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease">respiratory infections</a>, '
             '<a href="Pneumothorax" title="Pneumothorax">pneumothorax</a>, <a '
             'href="Interstitial_lung_disease" title="Interstitial lung disease">interstitial '
             'emphysema</a>, <a href="Pulmonary_heart_disease" title="Pulmonary heart '
             'disease">pulmonary heart disease</a>, and <a href="Respiratory_acidosis" '
             'title="Respiratory acidosis">respiratory acidosis</a>. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Paraseptal">Paraseptal</h3></div> <p>Paraseptal emphysema, also '
             'called <b>distal acinar emphysema</b>, relates to emphysematous change next to a <a '
             'href="Pleura" title="Pleura">pleural</a> surface, or to a <a href="Lung#Right_lung" '
             'title="Lung">fissure</a>. The cystic spaces known as <a '
             'href="Focal_lung_pneumatosis" title="Focal lung pneumatosis">blebs or bullae</a> '
             'that form in paraseptal emphysema typically occur in just one layer beneath the '
             'pleura. This distinguishes it from the <a href="Honeycombing" '
             'title="Honeycombing">honeycombing</a> of small cystic spaces seen in <a '
             'href="Fibrosis" title="Fibrosis">fibrosis</a> that typically occurs in layers. This '
             'type of emphysema is not associated with airflow obstruction. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Bullous">Bullous</h4></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="772" data-file-width="1019" decoding="async" height="189" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Bullus_emphasemaCT.png" width="250"/><figcaption>CT scan of <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Bullous_emphysema" title="Bullous emphysema">bullous '
             'emphysema</a></figcaption></figure> <p>When the <a href="Focal_lung_pneumatosis" '
             'title="Focal lung pneumatosis">subpleural bullae</a> are significant, the emphysema '
             'is called <b>bullous emphysema</b>. Bullae can become extensive and combine to form '
             'giant bullae. These can be large enough to take up a third of a hemithorax, compress '
             'the lung parenchyma, and cause displacement. The emphysema is now termed <b>giant '
             'bullous emphysema</b>, more commonly called <b>vanishing lung syndrome</b> due to '
             'the compressed parenchyma. A <a href="Focal_lung_pneumatosis" title="Focal lung '
             'pneumatosis">bleb or bulla</a> may sometimes rupture and cause a <a '
             'href="Pneumothorax" title="Pneumothorax">pneumothorax</a>. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Paracicatricial">Paracicatricial</h3></div> '
             '<p>Paracicatricial emphysema, also known as irregular emphysema, is seen next to '
             'areas of <a href="Fibrosis" title="Fibrosis">fibrosis</a> (scarring) as large '
             'spaces. The scarring is most often a result of <a href="Silicosis" '
             'title="Silicosis">silicosis</a>, <a href="Granuloma" '
             'title="Granuloma">granulomatous</a> infection, <a href="Tuberculosis" '
             'title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a>, or <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Pulmonary_infarction" title="Pulmonary infarction">pulmonary infarction</a>. '
             'It can be difficult to differentiate from the <a href="Honeycombing" '
             'title="Honeycombing">honeycombing</a> of <a href="Pulmonary_fibrosis" '
             'title="Pulmonary fibrosis">pulmonary fibrosis</a>. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="HIV_associated">HIV associated</h3></div> <p>Classic lung '
             'diseases are a complication of <a href="HIV/AIDS" title="HIV/AIDS">HIV/AIDS</a> with '
             'emphysema being a source of disease. HIV is cited as a risk factor for the '
             'development of emphysema and COPD regardless of smoking status. Around 20 percent of '
             'those with HIV have increased emphysematous changes. This has suggested that an '
             'underlying mechanism related to HIV is a contributory factor in the development of '
             'emphysema. HIV associated emphysema occurs over a much shorter time than that '
             'associated with smoking; an earlier presentation is also seen in emphysema caused by '
             '<a href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin_deficiency" title="Alpha-1 antitrypsin '
             'deficiency">alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency</a>. Both of these conditions '
             'predominantly show damage in the lower lungs, which suggests a similarity between '
             'the two mechanisms. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Alpha-1_related">Alpha-1 related</h3></div> <p>Emphysema may develop in some '
             'people with <a href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin_deficiency" title="Alpha-1 antitrypsin '
             'deficiency">alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency</a>, the only <a '
             'href="Chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease#Subtypes" title="Chronic obstructive '
             'pulmonary disease">genotype of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease</a>. This '
             'usually occurs a lot earlier (as does HIV associated emphysema) than other types. '
             '</p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Ritalin_lung">Ritalin '
             'lung</h3></div> <p>The intravenous use of <a href="Methylphenidate" '
             'title="Methylphenidate">methylphenidate</a>, commonly marketed as <i>Ritalin</i> and '
             'widely used as a <a class="mw-redirect" href="Stimulant_drug" title="Stimulant '
             'drug">stimulant drug</a> in the treatment of <a '
             'href="Attention_deficit_hyperactivity_disorder" title="Attention deficit '
             'hyperactivity disorder">attention deficit hyperactivity disorder</a>, can lead to '
             'emphysematous changes known as <b>Ritalin lung</b>. The mechanism underlying this '
             'link is not clearly understood. Ritalin tablets are not intended to be injected. '
             'They contain <a href="Talc" title="Talc">talc</a> as a filler, and it has been '
             'suggested that talc exposure causes <a href="Granuloma" '
             'title="Granuloma">granulomatosis</a> leading to alveolar destruction. However, other '
             'intravenous drugs also contain talc, and no emphysematous change is associated with '
             'those. <a href="High-resolution_computed_tomography" title="High-resolution computed '
             'tomography">High resolution CT scanning</a> shows the emphysema to be panlobular. '
             '</p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="CPFE">CPFE</h3></div> <p><a '
             'href="Combined_pulmonary_fibrosis_and_emphysema" title="Combined pulmonary fibrosis '
             'and emphysema">Combined pulmonary fibrosis and emphysema</a> (CPFE) is a rare '
             'syndrome that shows upper-lobe emphysema, together with lower-lobe interstitial '
             'fibrosis. This is diagnosed by <a href="CT_scan" title="CT scan">CT scan</a>. This '
             'syndrome presents a marked susceptibility for the development of <a '
             'href="Pulmonary_hypertension" title="Pulmonary hypertension">pulmonary '
             'hypertension</a>. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="SRIF">SRIF</h3></div> <p><a href="Smoking-related_interstitial_fibrosis_(SRIF)" '
             'title="Smoking-related interstitial fibrosis (SRIF)">Smoking-related interstitial '
             'fibrosis (SRIF)</a> is another type of fibrosis that occurs in emphysematous lungs '
             'and can be identified by pathologists. Unlike CPFE, this type of fibrosis is usually '
             'clinically occult (i.e., does not cause symptoms or imaging abnormalities). '
             'Occasionally, however, some patients with SRIF present with symptoms and radiologic '
             'findings of interstitial lung disease. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Congenital_lobar">Congenital lobar</h3></div> <p>Congenital lobar emphysema '
             '(CLE), also known as congenital lobar overinflation and infantile lobar emphysema, '
             'is a <a href="Pediatrics" title="Pediatrics">neonatal condition</a> associated with '
             'enlarged air spaces in the lungs of <a href="Infant" title="Infant">newborn '
             'infants</a>. It is diagnosed around the time of birth or in the first 6 months of '
             'life, occurring more often in boys than girls. CLE affects the upper <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Lung_lobes" title="Lung lobes">lung lobes</a> more than '
             'the lower lobes, and the left lung more often than the right lung. CLE is defined as '
             'the <a href="Inhalation#Hyperinflation" title="Inhalation">hyperinflation</a> of one '
             'or more lobes of the lung due to the partial obstruction of the bronchus. This '
             'causes symptoms of pressure on the nearby organs. It is associated with several '
             'cardiac abnormalities such as <a href="Patent_ductus_arteriosus" title="Patent '
             'ductus arteriosus">patent ductus arteriosus</a>, <a href="Atrial_septal_defect" '
             'title="Atrial septal defect">atrial septal defect</a>, <a '
             'href="Ventricular_septal_defect" title="Ventricular septal defect">ventricular '
             'septal defect</a>, and <a href="Tetralogy_of_Fallot" title="Tetralogy of '
             'Fallot">tetralogy of Fallot</a>. Although CLE may be caused by the abnormal '
             'development of <a href="Bronchus" title="Bronchus">bronchi</a>, or compression of '
             'airways by nearby tissues, no cause is identified in half of cases. CT scan of the '
             'lungs is useful in assessing the anatomy of the lung lobes and status of the '
             'neighbouring lobes on whether they are hypoplastic or not. Contrast-enhanced CT is '
             'useful in assessing vascular abnormalities and mediastinal masses. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Focal">Focal</h3></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="385" data-file-width="610" decoding="async" height="158" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Pneumot_rax_bullae.JPG" width="250"/><figcaption>A large bulla '
             'and a smaller bleb illustrated</figcaption></figure> <p>Focal emphysema is a '
             'localized region of emphysema in the lung that is larger than alveoli, and often '
             'associated with <a class="mw-redirect" href="Coalworker\'s_pneumoconiosis" '
             'title="Coalworker\'s pneumoconiosis">coalworker\'s pneumoconiosis</a>. This is also '
             'known as <i>localized pulmonary emphysema</i>. <a href="Focal_lung_pneumatosis" '
             'title="Focal lung pneumatosis">Blebs and bullae</a> may also be included as focal '
             'emphysema. These can be differentiated from the other type of enclosed air space '
             'known as a <a class="mw-redirect" href="Lung_cyst" title="Lung cyst">lung cyst</a> '
             'by their size and wall thickness. A bleb or bulla has a wall thickness of less than '
             '1\xa0mm, and are smaller. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 '
             'id="Occupational">Occupational</h4></div> <p>A number of occupations are associated '
             'with the development of emphysema due to the inhalation of varied gases and '
             'particles. In the US uranium mining that releases radon gas and particles has been '
             'shown to be a cause of emphysema deaths; the figures in the study included some '
             'miners who also smoked. Uranium mining and milling was found to create environmental '
             'pollution. </p><p>The inhalation of coal mine dust that can result in <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Coalworker\'s_pneumoconiosis" title="Coalworker\'s '
             'pneumoconiosis">coalworker\'s pneumoconiosis</a> is an independent risk factor for '
             'the development of emphysema. Focal emphysema is associated with the <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Coalworker\'s_pneumoconiosis" title="Coalworker\'s '
             'pneumoconiosis">coal macule</a>, and this extends into progressive centrilobular '
             'emphysema. Less commonly a variant of panlobular emphysema develops. </p><p><a '
             'href="Silicosis" title="Silicosis">Silicosis</a> results from the inhalation of '
             'silica particles, and the formation of large silica nodules is associated with '
             'paracicatricial emphysema, with or without bullae. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading4"><h4 id="Ozone-induced_emphysema">Ozone-induced emphysema</h4></div> '
             '<p>Ozone is another <a href="Pollutant" title="Pollutant">pollutant</a> that can '
             'affect the respiratory system. Long-term exposure to ozone can result in emphysema. '
             '</p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Osteoporosis">Osteoporosis</h2></div> <p><a href="Osteoporosis" '
             'title="Osteoporosis">Osteoporosis</a> is a major <a href="Comorbidity" '
             'title="Comorbidity">comorbidity</a> of emphysema. Both conditions are associated '
             'with a low <a href="Body_mass_index" title="Body mass index">body mass index</a>. '
             'There is an association between treating emphysema and <a href="Osteoporosis" '
             'title="Osteoporosis">osteoporosis</a>; the use of <a href="Systemic_administration" '
             'title="Systemic administration">systemic</a> <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Corticosteroids" title="Corticosteroids">corticosteroids</a> for treating <a '
             'href="Acute_exacerbation_of_chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Acute '
             'exacerbation of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease">exacerbations</a> is a '
             'significant risk factor for osteoporosis, and their repeated use is not recommended. '
             '</p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Other_terms">Other terms</h2></div> '
             '<div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Further information: <a '
             'href="Pneumatosis" title="Pneumatosis">Pneumatosis</a></div> <p><b>Compensatory '
             'emphysema</b> is overinflation of part of a lung in response to either removal by '
             'surgery of another part of the lung or decreased size of another part of the lung. '
             '</p><p><a href="Pulmonary_interstitial_emphysema" title="Pulmonary interstitial '
             'emphysema">Pulmonary interstitial emphysema</a> (PIE) is a collection of air inside '
             'the lungs but outside the normal air space of the <a href="Pulmonary_alveolus" '
             'title="Pulmonary alveolus">alveoli</a>, found as <a href="Pneumatosis" '
             'title="Pneumatosis">pneumatoses</a> inside the <a href="Connective_tissue" '
             'title="Connective tissue">connective tissue</a> of the peribronchovascular sheaths, '
             'interlobular septa, and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Pulmonary_pleurae" '
             'title="Pulmonary pleurae">visceral pleura</a>. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Lung_volume_reduction">Lung volume reduction</h2></div> '
             '<p><b>Lung volume reduction</b> may be offered to those with advanced emphysema. '
             'When other treatments fail, and the emphysema is in the upper lobes, a surgical '
             'option may be possible. A number of minimally invasive <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Bronchoscopic" title="Bronchoscopic">bronchoscopic</a> procedures are '
             'increasingly used to reduce lung volume. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Surgical">Surgical</h3></div> <p>Where there is severe '
             'emphysema with significant hyperinflation that has proved unresponsive to other '
             'therapies, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Lung_volume_reduction_surgery" title="Lung '
             'volume reduction surgery">lung volume reduction surgery</a> (LVRS) may be an option. '
             'LVRS involves the removal of tissue from the lobe most damaged by emphysema, which '
             'allows the other lobes to expand and give improved function. The procedure appears '
             'to be particularly effective if the emphysema primarily involves the upper lobes; '
             'however, the procedure increases the risk of adverse events and early death in '
             'people who have diffuse emphysema. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Bronchoscopic">Bronchoscopic</h3></div> <p>Minimally invasive bronchoscopic '
             'procedures may be carried out to reduce lung volume. These include the use of '
             'valves, coils, or thermal ablation. <a href="Endobronchial_valve" '
             'title="Endobronchial valve">Endobronchial valves</a> are one-way valves that may be '
             'used in those with severe hyperinflation resulting from advanced emphysema; a '
             'suitable target lobe and no <a href="Collateral_ventilation" title="Collateral '
             'ventilation">collateral ventilation</a> are required for this procedure. The '
             'placement of one or more valves in the lobe induces a partial <a href="Atelectasis" '
             'title="Atelectasis">collapse</a> of the lobe that ensures a reduction in residual '
             'volume that improves lung function, the capacity for exercise, and quality of life. '
             '</p><p>The placement of endobronchial coils made of nitinol, instead of valves is '
             'recommended where there is collateral ventilation that would prevent the use of '
             'valves. Nitinol is a <a href="Nitinol_biocompatibility" title="Nitinol '
             'biocompatibility">biocompatible</a> shape-memory alloy. </p><p>Both of these '
             'techniques are associated with adverse effects, including persistent air leaks and '
             'cardiovascular complications. Bronchoscopic thermal vapor ablation has an improved '
             'profile. Heated water vapor is used to target affected lobe regions, which leads to '
             'permanent fibrosis and volume reduction. The procedure is able to target individual '
             'lobe segments, can be carried out regardless of collateral ventilation, and can be '
             'repeated with the natural advance of emphysema. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Other_surgeries">Other surgeries</h2></div> <p><a '
             'href="Lung_transplantation" title="Lung transplantation">Lung transplantation</a> – '
             'the replacement of either a single lung or both (bilateral) – may be considered in '
             '<a href="End_stage_pulmonary_disease" title="End stage pulmonary disease">end-stage '
             'disease</a>. A bilateral transplant is the preferred choice as complications can '
             'arise in a remaining single native lung; complications can include hyperinflation, '
             'pneumonia, and the development of lung cancer. Careful selection as recommended by '
             'the <i>National Emphysema Treatment Trial</i> (NETT) for transplant surgeries is '
             'needed as in some cases there will be an increased risk of mortality. Several '
             'factors, including age and exercise tolerance using the <a href="BODE_index" '
             'title="BODE index">BODE index</a> need to be taken into account. A transplant is '
             'considered only when there are no serious comorbidites. A <a href="CT_scan" '
             'title="CT scan">CT scan</a> or a <a href="Ventilation/perfusion_scan" '
             'title="Ventilation/perfusion scan">ventilation/perfusion scan</a> may be useful to '
             'evaluate cases for surgical interventions and to evaluate post-surgery responses. A '
             '<a href="Bullectomy" title="Bullectomy">bullectomy</a> may be carried out when a '
             'giant bulla occupies more than a third of a hemithorax. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="In_other_tissues">In other tissues</h2></div> <p>Trapped air '
             'can also develop in other tissues such as under the skin, known as <a '
             'href="Subcutaneous_emphysema" title="Subcutaneous emphysema">subcutaneous '
             'emphysema</a>. Orbital emphysema is the trapping of air in the <a '
             'href="Orbit_(anatomy)" title="Orbit (anatomy)">orbit</a>; a type of this is '
             'palpebral emphysema that affects just the eyelids. Emphysematous <a href="Gastritis" '
             'title="Gastritis">gastritis</a> is the presence of air in the stomach wall, usually '
             'caused by a bacterial infection. This is rare but has a high mortality rate. </p> '
             '<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="History">History</h2></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="292" data-file-width="200" decoding="async" height="219" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Giambattista_morgagni.gif" width="150"/><figcaption>Giovanni '
             'Battista Morgagni, who recorded one of the earliest descriptions of emphysema in '
             '1769</figcaption></figure> <p>The terms <i>emphysema</i> and <i>chronic '
             'bronchitis</i> were formally defined in 1959 at the <a href="Novartis" '
             'title="Novartis">CIBA</a> guest symposium, and in 1962 at the <a '
             'href="American_Thoracic_Society" title="American Thoracic Society">American Thoracic '
             'Society</a> Committee meeting on Diagnostic Standards. The word <i>emphysema</i> is '
             "derived from Ancient Greek ἐμφύσημα 'inflation, swelling' (referring to a lung "
             'inflated by air-filled spaces), itself from <span title="Greek-language text"><span '
             'lang="el">ἐμφυσάω</span></span> <i>emphysao</i> \'to blow in, to inflate\', composed '
             'of ἐν <i>en</i>, meaning "<i>in</i>", and φυσᾶ <i>physa</i>, meaning "<i>wind, '
             'blast</i><span style="padding-left:.15em;">"</span>. </p><p>René Laennec, the '
             'physician who invented the <a href="Stethoscope" '
             'title="Stethoscope">stethoscope</a>, used the term <i>emphysema</i> in his book <i>A '
             'Treatise on the Diseases of the Chest and of Mediate Auscultation</i> (1837) to '
             'describe lungs that did not collapse when he opened the chest during an autopsy. He '
             'noted that they did not collapse as usual because they were full of air and the '
             'airways were filled with mucus. Early descriptions of probable emphysema include: in '
             '1679 by T. Bonet of a condition of "voluminous lungs" and in 1769 by Giovanni '
             'Morgagni of lungs which were "turgid particularly from air". In 1721 the first '
             'drawings of emphysema were made by Ruysh. These were followed the illustrations of '
             'Matthew Baillie in 1789 and descriptions of the destructive nature of the condition. '
             '</p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2">',
  'heading': 'Emphysema',
  'url': 'http://localhost:821/wikipedia_en_medicine_maxi_2025-09/A/Emphysema'}]

             'class="mw-page-container"> <div class="mw-page-container-inner"> <div '
