             'width="120"/></span></div> <div class="gallerytext">Very severe emphysema with lung '
             'cancer on the left (CT scan)</div> </li> </ul> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Differential_diagnosis">Differential diagnosis</h3></div> '
             '<p>COPD may need to be <a href="Differential_diagnosis" title="Differential '
             'diagnosis">differentiated</a> from other conditions such as <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Congestive_heart_failure" title="Congestive heart failure">congestive heart '
             'failure</a>, <a href="Asthma" title="Asthma">asthma</a>, <a href="Bronchiectasis" '
             'title="Bronchiectasis">bronchiectasis</a>, <a href="Tuberculosis" '
             'title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a>, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Obliterative_bronchiolitis" title="Obliterative bronchiolitis">obliterative '
             'bronchiolitis</a>, <a href="Lung_cancer" title="Lung cancer">lung cancer</a>, and <a '
             'href="Diffuse_panbronchiolitis" title="Diffuse panbronchiolitis">diffuse '
             'panbronchiolitis</a>. The distinction between asthma and COPD is made based on the '
             'symptoms, smoking history and whether airflow limitation is reversible with '
             'bronchodilators at spirometry. Chronic bronchitis with normal airflow is not '
             'classified as COPD. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Prevention">Prevention</h2></div> <p>Most cases of COPD are potentially '
             'preventable through decreasing exposure to tobacco smoke and other indoor and '
             'outdoor pollutants. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Smoking_cessation">Smoking cessation</h3></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">See also: <a href="World_No_Tobacco_Day" '
             'title="World No Tobacco Day">World No Tobacco Day</a></div> <p>The <a '
             'href="Tobacco_smoking#Legal_issues_and_regulation" title="Tobacco '
             'smoking">policies</a> of governments, public health agencies and antismoking '
             'organizations can reduce smoking rates by discouraging people from starting and '
             'encouraging people to stop smoking. <a href="Smoking_ban" title="Smoking '
             'ban">Smoking bans</a> in public areas and places of work are important measures to '
             'decrease exposure to secondhand smoke, and while many places have instituted bans, '
             'more are recommended. </p><p>In those who smoke, <a href="Smoking_cessation" '
             'title="Smoking cessation">stopping smoking</a> is the only measure shown to slow '
             'down the worsening of COPD. Even at a late stage of the disease, it can reduce the '
             'rate of worsening lung function and delay the onset of disability and death. Often, '
             'several attempts are required before long-term abstinence is achieved. Attempts over '
             '5 years lead to success in nearly 40% of people. </p><p>Some smokers can achieve '
             'long-term smoking cessation through willpower alone. Smoking, however, is highly '
             'addictive, and many smokers need further support. The chance of quitting is improved '
             'with social support, engagement in a smoking cessation program and the use of '
             'medications such as <a href="Nicotine_replacement_therapy" title="Nicotine '
             'replacement therapy">nicotine replacement therapy</a>, <a href="Bupropion" '
             'title="Bupropion">bupropion</a>, or <a href="Varenicline" '
             'title="Varenicline">varenicline</a>. Combining smoking-cessation medication with '
             'behavioral therapy is more than twice as likely to be effective in helping people '
             'with COPD stop smoking, compared with behavioral therapy alone. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Occupational_health">Occupational '
             'health</h3></div> <p>Several measures have been taken to reduce the likelihood that '
             'workers in at-risk industries—such as coal mining, construction and '
             'stonemasonry—will develop COPD. Examples of these measures include the creation of '
             'public policy, education of workers and management about the risks, promoting '
             'smoking cessation, <a href="Disease_surveillance" title="Disease '
             'surveillance">checking</a> workers for early signs of COPD, use of <a '
             'href="Respirator" title="Respirator">respirators</a> and dust control. Effective '
             'dust control can be achieved by improving ventilation, using water sprays and by '
             'using mining techniques that minimize dust generation. If a worker develops COPD, '
             'further lung damage can be reduced by avoiding ongoing dust exposure, for example by '
             'changing their work role. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Pollution_control">Pollution control</h3></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Further information: Indoor air pollution in '
             'developing nations and <a href="Air_pollution#Pollution_control" title="Air '
             'pollution">Air pollution §\xa0Pollution control</a></div> <p>Both indoor and outdoor '
             'air quality can be improved, which may prevent COPD or slow the worsening of '
             'existing disease. This may be achieved by public policy efforts, cultural changes '
             'and personal involvement. Many developed countries have successfully improved '
             'outdoor air quality through regulations, which has resulted in improvements in the '
             'lung function of their populations. Individuals are also advised to avoid irritants '
             'of indoor and outdoor pollution. </p><p>In developing countries, one key effort is '
             'to reduce exposure to smoke from cooking and heating fuels through improved '
             'ventilation of homes and better stoves and chimneys. Proper stoves may improve '
             'indoor air quality by 85%. Using alternative energy sources such as solar cooking '
             'and electrical heating is also effective. Using fuels such as kerosene or coal might '
             'produce less household particulate matter than traditional biomass such as wood or '
             'dung, but whether this is better health wise is unclear. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Management">Management</h2></div> <p>COPD currently has no '
             'cure, but the symptoms are treatable and its progression can be delayed, '
             'particularly by <a href="Smoking_cessation" title="Smoking cessation">stopping '
             'smoking</a>. The major goals of management are to reduce exposure to risk factors '
             'including offering non-pharmacological treatments such as help with stopping '
             'smoking. Stopping smoking can reduce the rate of lung function decline and also '
             'reduce mortality from smoking-related diseases such as lung cancer and '
             'cardiovascular disease. Other recommendations include <a href="Pneumococcal_vaccine" '
             'title="Pneumococcal vaccine">pneumococcal vaccination</a> and yearly <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Influenza_vaccination" title="Influenza '
             'vaccination">influenza vaccination</a> to help reduce the risk of exacerbations; as '
             'of 2024 CDC and GOLD also recommend <a class="mw-redirect" href="RSV_vaccine" '
             'title="RSV vaccine">RSV vaccine</a> for individuals above 60 years. Guidance is also '
             'advised as to managing breathlessness and stress. </p><p><a href="Comorbidity" '
             'title="Comorbidity">Other illnesses</a> are also being managed. An action plan is '
             'drawn up and is to be reviewed. Providing people with a personalized action plan, an '
             'educational session and support for use of their action plan in the event of an '
             'exacerbation, reduces the number of hospital visits and encourages early treatment '
             'of exacerbations. When self-management interventions, such as taking corticosteroids '
             'and using supplemental oxygen, are combined with action plans, health-related '
             'quality of life is improved compared to usual care. In those with COPD who are <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Malnourished" title="Malnourished">malnourished</a>, '
             'supplementation with <a href="Vitamin_C" title="Vitamin C">vitamin C</a>, <a '
             'href="Vitamin_E" title="Vitamin E">vitamin E</a>, <a href="Zinc" '
             'title="Zinc">zinc</a> and selenium can improve weight, strength of <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Respiratory_muscles" title="Respiratory '
             'muscles">respiratory muscles</a> and health-related quality of life. Significant <a '
             'href="Vitamin_D_deficiency" title="Vitamin D deficiency">vitamin D deficiency</a> is '
             'common in those with COPD and can cause increased exacerbations. Supplementation '
             'when deficient can give a 50% reduction in the number of exacerbations. '
             '</p><p>Several <a href="Pharmacotherapy" title="Pharmacotherapy">medical '
             'treatments</a> are used in the management of stable COPD and exacerbations. These '
             'include <a href="Bronchodilator" title="Bronchodilator">bronchodilators</a>, <a '
             'href="Corticosteroid" title="Corticosteroid">corticosteroids</a> and <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Antibiotics" title="Antibiotics">antibiotics</a>. '
             '</p><p>In those with a severe exacerbation, antibiotics improve outcomes. Several '
             'different antibiotics may be used, including <a href="Amoxicillin" '
             'title="Amoxicillin">amoxicillin</a>, <a href="Doxycycline" '
             'title="Doxycycline">doxycycline</a> and <a href="Azithromycin" '
             'title="Azithromycin">azithromycin</a>; whether one is better than the others is '
             'unclear. There is no clear evidence of improved outcomes for those with less severe '
             'cases. The FDA recommends against the use of <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Fluoroquinolones" title="Fluoroquinolones">fluoroquinolones</a> when other '
             'options are available due to higher risks of serious side effects. In treating <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Acute_hypercapnic_respiratory_failure" title="Acute '
             'hypercapnic respiratory failure">acute hypercapnic respiratory failure</a> (acutely '
             'raised levels of carbon dioxide), <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Bilevel_positive_airway_pressure" title="Bilevel positive airway '
             'pressure">bilevel positive airway pressure</a> (BPAP) can decrease mortality and the '
             'need of <a href="Intensive_care_medicine" title="Intensive care medicine">intensive '
             'care</a>. </p><p>In those with <a href="End_stage_pulmonary_disease" title="End '
             'stage pulmonary disease">end-stage disease</a>, <a href="Palliative_care" '
             'title="Palliative care">palliative care</a> is focused on relieving symptoms. <a '
             'href="Morphine" title="Morphine">Morphine</a> can improve exercise tolerance. <a '
             'href="Non-invasive_ventilation" title="Non-invasive ventilation">Non-invasive '
             'ventilation</a> may be used to support breathing and also reduce daytime '
             'breathlessness. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Bronchodilators">Bronchodilators</h3></div> <p>Inhaled short-acting <a '
             'href="Bronchodilator" title="Bronchodilator">bronchodilators</a> are the primary '
             'medications used on an <a href="Pro_re_nata" title="Pro re nata">as needed</a> '
             'basis; their use regularly is not recommended. The two major types are <a '
             'href="Beta2-adrenergic_agonist" title="Beta2-adrenergic agonist">beta2-adrenergic '
             'agonists</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Anticholinergics" '
             'title="Anticholinergics">anticholinergics</a>; either in long-acting or short-acting '
             'forms. Beta2–adrenergic agonists target receptors in the <a href="Smooth_muscle" '
             'title="Smooth muscle">smooth muscle cells</a> in <a href="Bronchiole" '
             'title="Bronchiole">bronchioles</a> causing them to relax and allow improved airflow. '
             'They reduce shortness of breath, tend to reduce <a href="Dynamic_hyperinflation" '
             'title="Dynamic hyperinflation">dynamic hyperinflation</a> and improve exercise '
             'tolerance. Short-acting bronchodilators have an effect for four hours and for '
             'maintenance therapy long acting bronchodilators with an effect of over twelve hours '
             'are used. In times of more severe symptoms, a short-acting agent may be used in '
             'combination. An inhaled corticosteroid used with a long-acting beta-2 agonist is '
             'more effective than either one on its own. </p><p>Which type of long-acting agent, '
             '<a class="mw-redirect" href="Long-acting_muscarinic_antagonist" title="Long-acting '
             'muscarinic antagonist">long-acting muscarinic antagonist</a> (LAMA) such as <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Tiotropium" title="Tiotropium">tiotropium</a> or <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Long-acting_beta_agonist" title="Long-acting beta '
             'agonist">long-acting beta agonist</a> (LABA), is better is unclear, and trying each '
             'and continuing with the one that works best may be advisable. Both types of agent '
             'appear to reduce the risk of acute exacerbations by 15–25%. The combination of '
             'LABA/LAMA may reduce COPD exacerbations and improve quality-of-life compared to '
             'long-acting bronchodilators alone. The 2018 NICE guideline recommends use of dual '
             'long-acting bronchodilators with economic modelling suggesting that this approach is '
             'preferable to starting one long-acting bronchodilator and adding another later. '
             '</p><p>Several short-acting β agonists are available, including <a href="Salbutamol" '
             'title="Salbutamol">salbutamol</a> (albuterol) and <a href="Terbutaline" '
             'title="Terbutaline">terbutaline</a>. They provide relief of symptoms for four to six '
             'hours. A <a class="mw-redirect" href="Long-acting_beta_agonist" title="Long-acting '
             'beta agonist">long-acting beta agonist</a> (LABA) such as <a href="Salmeterol" '
             'title="Salmeterol">salmeterol</a>, <a href="Formoterol" '
             'title="Formoterol">formoterol</a> and <a href="Indacaterol" '
             'title="Indacaterol">indacaterol</a> are often used as maintenance therapy, with a '
             'duration of action of 12 to 24 hours. Some feel the evidence of benefits is limited, '
             'while others view the evidence of benefit as established. Long-term use of LABAs '
             'appears safe in COPD, with adverse effects include <a href="Tremor" '
             'title="Tremor">shakiness</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Palpitation" '
             'title="Palpitation">heart palpitations</a>. When used with inhaled steroids they '
             'increase the risk of pneumonia. While steroids and LABAs may work better together, '
             'it is unclear if this slight benefit outweighs the increased risks. There is some '
             'evidence that combined treatment of LABAs with long-acting <a '
             'href="Muscarinic_antagonist" title="Muscarinic antagonist">muscarinic '
             'antagonists</a> (LAMA), an anticholinergic, and LABA +ICS (inhaled corticosteroid) '
             "may be similar in benefits in terms of fewer exacerbation's and quality of life "
             'measures for moderate to severe COPD, but LAMA+LABA offers better improvements in '
             'forced expiratory volume (<a class="mw-redirect" href="FEV1%25" '
             'title="FEV1%">FEV1%</a>) and a lower risk of pneumonia. All three together, LABA, '
             'LAMA and ICS, have some evidence of benefits. Indacaterol requires an inhaled dose '
             'once a day and is as effective as the other long-acting β agonist drugs that require '
             'twice-daily dosing for people with stable COPD. </p><p>The two main anticholinergics '
             'used in COPD are <a class="mw-redirect" href="Ipratropium" '
             'title="Ipratropium">ipratropium</a> and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Tiotropium" '
             'title="Tiotropium">tiotropium</a>. Ipratropium is a <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Short-acting_muscarinic_antagonist" title="Short-acting muscarinic '
             'antagonist">short-acting muscarinic antagonist</a> (SAMA), while tiotropium is <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Long-acting_muscarinic_antagonist" title="Long-acting '
             'muscarinic antagonist">long-acting muscarinic antagonist</a> (LAMA). Tiotropium is '
             'associated with a decrease in exacerbations and improved quality of life, and '
             'tiotropium provides those benefits better than ipratropium. Tiotropium does not '
             'appear to affect mortality or the overall hospitalization rate. Anticholinergics can '
             'cause dry mouth and urinary tract symptoms. They are also associated with increased '
             'risk of heart disease and <a href="Stroke" title="Stroke">stroke</a>. <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Aclidinium" title="Aclidinium">Aclidinium</a>, another '
             'long-acting agent, reduces hospitalizations associated with COPD and improves '
             'quality of life. The LAMA <a href="Umeclidinium_bromide" title="Umeclidinium '
             'bromide">umeclidinium bromide</a> is another anticholinergic alternative. When '
             'compared to tiotropium, the LAMAs aclidinium, glycopyrronium, and umeclidinium '
             'appear to have a similar level of efficacy, with all four being more effective than '
             '<a href="Placebo" title="Placebo">placebo</a>. Further research is needed comparing '
             'aclidinium to tiotropium. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Corticosteroids">Corticosteroids</h3></div> <p>Inhaled <a href="Corticosteroid" '
             'title="Corticosteroid">corticosteroids</a> are anti-inflammatories that are '
             'recommended by <a href="Global_Initiative_for_Chronic_Obstructive_Lung_Disease" '
             'title="Global Initiative for Chronic Obstructive Lung Disease">GOLD</a> as a '
             'first-line maintenance treatment in COPD cases with repeated exacerbations. Their '
             'regular use increases the risk of pneumonia in severe cases. Studies have shown that '
             'the risk of pneumonia is associated with all types of corticosteroids; is related to '
             'the disease severity and a dose-response relationship has been noted. Oral <a '
             'href="Glucocorticoid" title="Glucocorticoid">glucocorticoids</a> can be effective in '
             'treating an acute exacerbation. They appear to have fewer side effects than those '
             'given intravenously. Five days of steroids work as well as ten or fourteen days. '
             '</p><p>The use of corticosteroids is associated with a decrease in the number of '
             'lymphoid follicles (in the <a class="mw-redirect" href="BALT" title="BALT">bronchial '
             'lymphoid tissue</a>). A triple inhaled therapy of LABA/LAMA/ICS improves lung '
             'function, reduces symptoms and exacerbations and is seen to be more effective than '
             'mono or dual therapies. NICE guidelines recommend the use of ICSs in people with '
             'asthmatic features or features suggesting steroid responsiveness. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="PDE4_inhibitors">PDE4 inhibitors</h3></div> '
             '<p><a class="mw-redirect" href="Phosphodiesterase-4_inhibitor" '
             'title="Phosphodiesterase-4 inhibitor">Phosphodiesterase-4 inhibitors</a> (PDE4 '
             'inhibitors) are anti-inflammatories that improve lung function and reduce '
             'exacerbations in moderate to severe illness. <a href="Roflumilast" '
             'title="Roflumilast">Roflumilast</a> is a PDE4 inhibitor used orally once daily to '
             'reduce inflammation; it has no direct bronchodilatory effects. It is essentially '
             'used in treating those with chronic bronchitis along with systemic corticosteroids. '
             'Reported adverse effects of roflumilast appear early in treatment, become less with '
             'continued treatment and are reversible. One effect is dramatic weight loss, and its '
             'use is to be avoided in underweight people. It is also advised to be used with '
             'caution in those who have depression. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Other_medications">Other medications</h3></div> <p>Long-term <a '
             'href="Preventive_healthcare" title="Preventive healthcare">preventive use</a> of <a '
             'href="Antibiotic" title="Antibiotic">antibiotics</a>, specifically those from the <a '
             'href="Macrolide" title="Macrolide">macrolide</a> class such as <a '
             'href="Erythromycin" title="Erythromycin">erythromycin</a>, reduces the frequency of '
             'exacerbations in those who have two or more a year. This practice may be '
             'cost-effective in some areas of the world. Concerns include the potential for <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Antibiotic_resistance" title="Antibiotic '
             'resistance">antibiotic resistance</a> and side effects including <a '
             'href="Hearing_loss" title="Hearing loss">hearing loss</a>, <a href="Tinnitus" '
             'title="Tinnitus">tinnitus</a> and changes to the heart rhythm known as <a '
             'href="Drug-induced_QT_prolongation" title="Drug-induced QT prolongation">long QT '
             'syndrome</a>. </p><p><a href="Methylxanthines" '
             'title="Methylxanthines">Methylxanthines</a> such as <a href="Theophylline" '
             'title="Theophylline">theophylline</a> are widely used. Theophylline is seen to have '
             'a mild bronchodilatory effect in stable COPD. Inspiratory muscle function is seen to '
             'be improved, but the causal effect is unclear. Theophylline is seen to improve '
             'breathlessness when used as an add-on to <a href="Salmeterol" '
             'title="Salmeterol">salmeterol</a>. All instances of improvement have been reported '
             'using sustained-release preparations. Methylxanthines are not recommended for use in '
             'exacerbations due to adverse effects. </p><p><a class="mw-redirect" href="Mucolytic" '
             'title="Mucolytic">Mucolytics</a> may help to reduce exacerbations in some people '
             'with chronic bronchitis, as noticed by fewer hospitalizations and fewer days of '
             'disability in one month. <a href="Erdosteine" title="Erdosteine">Erdosteine</a> is '
             'recommended by NICE. GOLD also supports the use of some mucolytics that are advised '
             'against when inhaled corticosteroids are being used and singles out erdosteine as '
             'having good effects regardless of corticosteroid use. Erdosteine also has '
             'antioxidant properties, but there is not enough evidence to support the general use '
             'of antioxidants. Erdosteine has been shown to significantly reduce the risk of '
             'exacerbations, shorten their duration and hospital stays. </p><p><a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Cough_medicine" title="Cough medicine">Cough medicines</a> '
             'are not recommended. <a href="Beta_blocker" title="Beta blocker">Beta blockers</a> '
             'are not contraindicated for those with COPD and should only be used where there is '
             'concomitant cardiovascular disease. </p><p>Recent studies show that metformin plays '
             'a role in reducing systemic inflammation by reducing biomarker levels that are '
             'increased during COPD exacerbations. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Oxygen_therapy">Oxygen therapy</h3></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Further information: <a '
             'href="Effect_of_oxygen_on_chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Effect of '
             'oxygen on chronic obstructive pulmonary disease">Effect of oxygen on chronic '
             'obstructive pulmonary disease</a></div> <p><a href="Oxygen_therapy" title="Oxygen '
             'therapy">Supplemental oxygen</a> is recommended for those with <a href="Hypoxemia" '
             'title="Hypoxemia">low oxygen levels</a> in <a href="Respiratory_failure" '
             'title="Respiratory failure">respiratory failure</a> at rest (a <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Partial_pressure_of_oxygen" title="Partial pressure of '
             'oxygen">partial pressure of oxygen</a> less than 50–55\xa0mmHg or <a '
             'href="Oxygen_saturation_(medicine)" title="Oxygen saturation (medicine)">oxygen '
             'saturations</a> of less than 88%). When taking into account complications including '
             '<a class="mw-redirect" href="Cor_pulmonale" title="Cor pulmonale">cor pulmonale</a> '
             'and pulmonary hypertension, the levels involved are 56–59 mmHg. Oxygen therapy is to '
             'be used for between 15 and 18 hours per day and is said to decrease the risk of <a '
             'href="Heart_failure" title="Heart failure">heart failure</a> and death. In those '
             'with normal or mildly low oxygen levels, oxygen supplementation (ambulatory) may '
             'improve shortness of breath when given during exercise, but may not improve '
             'breathlessness during normal daily activities or affect the quality of life. During '
             'acute exacerbations, many require oxygen therapy; the use of high concentrations of '
             "oxygen without taking into account a person's oxygen saturations may lead to "
             'increased levels of carbon dioxide and worsened outcomes. In those at high risk of '
             'high carbon dioxide levels, oxygen saturations of 88–92% are recommended, while for '
             'those without this risk, recommended levels are 94–98%. Once prescribed long-term '
             'oxygen therapy, patients should be re-assessed after 60 to 90 days, to determine '
             'whether supplemental oxygen is still indicated and if prescribed supplemental oxygen '
             'is effective. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Rehabilitation">Rehabilitation</h3></div> <p><a href="Pulmonary_rehabilitation" '
             'title="Pulmonary rehabilitation">Pulmonary rehabilitation</a> is a program of '
             'exercise, disease management and counseling, coordinated to benefit the individual. '
             'A severe exacerbation leads to hospital admission, high mortality and a decline in '
             'the ability to carry out daily activities. Following a hospital admission, pulmonary '
             'rehabilitation has been shown to significantly reduce future hospital admissions, '
             'mortality and improve quality of life. </p><p>The optimal exercise routine, use of '
             'noninvasive ventilation during exercise and intensity of exercise suggested for '
             'people with COPD, is unknown. Performing endurance arm exercises improves arm '
             'movement for people with COPD and may result in a small improvement in '
             'breathlessness. Performing arm exercises alone does not appear to improve quality of '
             'life. <a href="Pursed-lip_breathing" title="Pursed-lip breathing">Pursed-lip '
             'breathing</a> exercises may be useful. <i>Tai chi</i> exercises appear to be safe to '
             'practice for people with COPD and may be beneficial for pulmonary function and '
             'pulmonary capacity when compared to a regular treatment program. Tai Chi was not '
             'found to be more effective than other exercise intervention programs. Inspiratory '
             'and expiratory muscle training (IMT, EMT) have been suggested and may provide some '
             'improvements when compared to no treatment. A combination of IMT and walking '
             'exercises at home may help limit breathlessness in cases of severe COPD. '
             'Additionally, the use of low amplitude high velocity joint mobilization together '
             'with exercise improves lung function and exercise capacity. The goal of spinal '
             'manipulation therapy is to improve thoracic mobility in an effort to reduce the work '
             'on the lungs during respiration, however, the evidence supporting manual therapy for '
             'people with COPD is very weak. </p><p><a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Airway_clearance_technique" title="Airway clearance technique">Airway '
             'clearance techniques</a> (ACTs), such as <a href="Postural_drainage" title="Postural '
             'drainage">postural drainage</a>, percussion/vibration, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Autogenic_drainage" title="Autogenic drainage">autogenic drainage</a>, '
             'hand-held <a class="mw-redirect" href="Positive_expiratory_pressure" title="Positive '
             'expiratory pressure">positive expiratory pressure</a> (PEP) devices and other '
             'mechanical devices, may reduce the need for increased ventilatory assistance, the '
             'duration of ventilatory assistance and the length of hospital stay in people with '
             'acute COPD. In people with stable COPD, ACTs may lead to short-term improvements in '
             'health-related quality of life and a reduced long-term need for hospitalizations '
             'related to respiratory issues. </p><p>Being either underweight or overweight can '
             'affect the symptoms, degree of disability and prognosis of COPD. People with COPD '
             'who are underweight can improve their breathing muscle strength by increasing their '
             'calorie intake. When combined with regular exercise or a pulmonary rehabilitation '
             'program, this can lead to improvements in COPD symptoms. Supplemental nutrition may '
             'be useful in those who are <a href="Malnutrition" '
             'title="Malnutrition">malnourished</a>. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Management_of_exacerbations">Management of exacerbations</h3></div> <p>People '
             'with COPD can experience <a '
             'href="Acute_exacerbation_of_chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Acute '
             'exacerbation of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease">exacerbations</a> (flare-ups) '
             'that are commonly caused by <a href="Respiratory_tract" title="Respiratory '
             'tract">respiratory tract</a> infections. The symptoms that worsen are not specific '
             'to COPD and differential diagnoses need to be considered. Acute exacerbations are '
             'typically treated by increasing the use of short-acting bronchodilators, including a '
             'combination of a short-acting inhaled beta agonist and short-acting anticholinergic. '
             'These medications can be given either via a <a href="Metered-dose_inhaler" '
             'title="Metered-dose inhaler">metered-dose inhaler</a> with a <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Asthma_spacer" title="Asthma spacer">spacer</a> or via a <a href="Nebulizer" '
             'title="Nebulizer">nebulizer</a>, with both appearing to be equally effective. '
             'Nebulization may be easier for those who are more unwell. <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Oxygen_supplementation" title="Oxygen supplementation">Oxygen '
             'supplementation</a> can be useful. Excessive oxygen; however, can result in <a '
             'href="Hypercapnia" title="Hypercapnia">increased CO levels</a> and a decreased level '
             'of consciousness. Corticosteroids <a href="Systemic_administration" title="Systemic '
             'administration">given orally</a> can improve lung function and shorten hospital '
             'stays but their use is recommended for only five to seven days; longer courses '
             'increase the risk of pneumonia and death. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Room_temperature">Room temperature</h3></div> <p>Maintaining '
             'room temperature of at least 21\xa0°C (70\xa0°F) for a minimum of nine hours a day '
             'was associated with better health in those with COPD, especially for smokers. The '
             'World Health Organization (WHO) recommends indoor temperatures of a slightly higher '
             'range between 18 and 24\xa0°C (64 and 75\xa0°F). </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Room_humidity">Room humidity</h3></div> <p>For people with '
             'COPD, the ideal indoor humidity levels are 30–50% RH. Maintaining indoor humidity '
             'can be difficult in the winter, especially in cold climates where the heating system '
             'is constantly running. </p><p>Keeping the indoor relative humidity above 40% RH '
             'significantly reduces the infectivity of aerosolized viruses. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Procedures_for_emphysema">Procedures for '
             'emphysema</h2></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main '
             'article: <a class="mw-redirect" href="Lung_volume_reduction" title="Lung volume '
             'reduction">Lung volume reduction</a></div> <p>There are several procedures to <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Lung_volume_reduction" title="Lung volume '
             'reduction">reduce the volume of a lung</a> in cases of severe emphysema with '
             'hyperinflation. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Surgical">Surgical</h3></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" '
             'role="note">Main article: <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Lung_volume_reduction_surgery" title="Lung volume reduction surgery">Lung '
             'volume reduction surgery</a></div> <p>For severe emphysema that has proved '
             'unresponsive to other therapies <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Lung_volume_reduction_surgery" title="Lung volume reduction surgery">lung '
             'volume reduction surgery</a> (LVRS) may be an option. LVRS involves the removal of '
             'damaged tissue, which improves lung function by allowing the rest of the lungs to '
             'expand. It is considered when the emphysema is in the upper lobes and when there are '
             'no comorbidities. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Bronchoscopic">Bronchoscopic</h3></div> <p>Minimally invasive bronchoscopic '
             'procedures may be carried out to reduce lung volume. These include the use of '
             'valves, coils, or thermal ablation. <a href="Endobronchial_valve" '
             'title="Endobronchial valve">Endobronchial valves</a> are one-way valves that may be '
             'used in those with severe hyperinflation resulting from advanced emphysema; a '
             'suitable target lobe and no <a href="Collateral_ventilation" title="Collateral '
             'ventilation">collateral ventilation</a> are required for this procedure. The '
             'placement of one or more valves in the lobe induces a partial <a href="Atelectasis" '
             'title="Atelectasis">collapse</a> of the lobe that ensures a reduction in residual '
             'volume that improves lung function, the capacity for exercise and quality of life. '
             '</p><p>The placement of nitinol coils instead of valves is recommended where there '
             'is collateral ventilation that would prevent the use of valves. Nitinol is a <a '
             'href="Nitinol_biocompatibility" title="Nitinol biocompatibility">biocompatible '
             'alloy</a>. </p><p>Both of these techniques are associated with adverse effects, '
             'including persistent air leaks and cardiovascular complications. Thermal vapor '
             'ablation has an improved profile. Heated water vapor is used to target lobe regions, '
             'which leads to permanent fibrosis and volume reduction. The procedure can target '
             'individual lobe segments, can be carried out regardless of collateral ventilation, '
             'and can be repeated with the natural advance of emphysema. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Other_surgeries">Other surgeries</h3></div> '
             '<p>In very severe cases, <a href="Lung_transplantation" title="Lung '
             'transplantation">lung transplantation</a> might be considered. A CT scan may be '
             'useful in surgery considerations. <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Ventilation/perfusion_scintigraphy" title="Ventilation/perfusion '
             'scintigraphy">Ventilation/perfusion scintigraphy</a> is another imaging method that '
             'may be used to evaluate cases for surgical interventions and also to evaluate '
             'post-surgery responses. A <a href="Bullectomy" title="Bullectomy">bullectomy</a> may '
             'be carried out when a giant <a href="Focal_lung_pneumatosis" title="Focal lung '
             'pneumatosis">bulla</a> occupies more than a third of a hemithorax. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Prognosis">Prognosis</h2></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '

             '</span>\xa0376–1089</div></div></figcaption></figure> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="415" data-file-width="940" decoding="async" height="146" '

             'width="330"/><figcaption><a href="Disability-adjusted_life_year" '

             '<table class="col-begin" role="presentation"> <tbody><tr> <td class="col-break"> '

             'color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">\xa0'
             '</span>\xa0≤110</div> <div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" '

             '</span>\xa0220–330</div> <div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" '

             'print-color-adjust: exact;">\xa0</span>\xa0330–440</div> <div class="legend"><span '
             'class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#ffb000; '
             'color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">\xa0'
             '</span>\xa0440–550</div> <div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" '
             'style="background-color:#ff9a00; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; '
             'print-color-adjust: exact;">\xa0</span>\xa0550–660</div> </td> <td '
             'class="col-break"> <div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" '
             'style="background-color:#ff8400; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; '
             'print-color-adjust: exact;">\xa0</span>\xa0660–770</div> <div class="legend"><span '
             'class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#ff6e00; '
             'color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">\xa0'
             '</span>\xa0770–880</div> <div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" '
             'style="background-color:#ff5800; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; '
             'print-color-adjust: exact;">\xa0</span>\xa0880–990</div> <div class="legend"><span '
             'class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#ff4200; '
             'color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">\xa0'
             '</span>\xa0990–1100</div> <div class="legend"><span class="legend-color '
             'mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#ff2c00; '
             'color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">\xa0'
             '</span>\xa01100–1350</div> <div class="legend"><span class="legend-color '
             'mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#cb0000; '
             'color:white;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">\xa0'
             '</span>\xa0≥1350</div> </td></tr></tbody></table></div></figcaption></figure> '
             '<p>COPD is progressive and can lead to premature death. It is estimated that 3% of '
             '<a class="mw-redirect" href="Disability_adjusted_life_years" title="Disability '
             'adjusted life years">all disability</a> is related to COPD. The proportion of '
             'disability from COPD globally has decreased from 1990 to 2010 due to improved indoor '
             'air quality primarily in Asia. The overall number of years lived with disability '
             'from COPD, however, has increased. </p><p>There are many variables affecting the '
             'long-term outcome in COPD and GOLD recommends the use of a composite test (<a '
             'href="BODE_index" title="BODE index">BODE</a>) that includes the main variables of '
             '<a class="mw-redirect" href="Body-mass_index" title="Body-mass index">body-mass '
             'index</a>, obstruction of airways, <a class="mw-redirect" href="Dyspnea" '
             'title="Dyspnea">dyspnea</a> (breathlessness) and exercise and not just spirometry '
             'results. NICE recommends against the use of BODE for the prognosis assessment in '
             'stable COPD; factors such as exacerbations and <a href="Frailty_syndrome" '
             'title="Frailty syndrome">frailty</a> need to be considered. Other factors that '
             'contribute to a poor outcome include older age, <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Comorbidities" title="Comorbidities">comorbidities</a> such as lung cancer and '
             'cardiovascular disease and the number and severity of exacerbations needing hospital '
             'admission. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Epidemiology">Epidemiology</h2></div> <p>Estimates of prevalence have '
             'considerable variation due to differences in analytical and surveying approach and '
             'the choice of diagnostic criteria. An estimated 213\xa0million people had COPD in '
             '2021, corresponding to a global prevalence of 2.7%, whereas epidemiological studies '
             'indicated an estimation of 384\xa0million having COPD in 2010, corresponding to a '
             'global prevalence of 12%. The disease affects men and women. The increase in the '
             'developing world between 1970 and the 2000s is believed to be related to increasing '
             'rates of smoking in this region, an increasing population and an aging population '
             'due to fewer deaths from other causes such as infectious diseases. Some developed '
             'countries have seen increased rates, some have remained stable and some have seen a '
             'decrease in COPD prevalence. </p><p>Around three million people die of COPD each '
             'year. In some countries, mortality has decreased in men but increased in women. This '
             'is most likely due to rates of smoking in women and men becoming more similar. A '
             'higher rate of COPD is found in those over 40 years and this increases greatly with '
             'advancing age with the highest rate found in those over 60 years. Sex differences in '
             'the anatomy of the respiratory system include smaller airway lumens and thicker '
             'airway walls in women, which contribute to a greater severity of COPD symptoms like '
             'dyspnea and frequency of COPD exacerbation. </p><p>In the UK, three million people '
             'are reported to be affected by COPD\xa0– two million of these being undiagnosed. On '
             'average, the number of COPD-related deaths between 2007 and 2016 was 28,600. The '
             'estimated number of deaths due to occupational exposure was estimated to be about '
             '15% at around 4,000. In the United States in 2018, almost 15.7 million people had '
             'been diagnosed with COPD and it is estimated that millions more have not been '
             'diagnosed. </p><p>In 2011, there were approximately 730,000 hospitalizations in the '
             'United States for COPD. Globally, COPD in 2019 was the third-leading cause of death. '
             'In low-income countries, COPD does not appear in the Top 10 causes of death; in '
             'other income groups, it is in the Top 5. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="History">History</h2></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Further information: <a '
             'href="Emphysema#History" title="Emphysema">Emphysema §\xa0History</a>, and <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Chronic_bronchitis" title="Chronic bronchitis">Chronic '
             'bronchitis §\xa0History</a></div> <figure class="mw-default-size" '
             'typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="292" '
             'data-file-width="200" decoding="async" height="292" loading="lazy" '
             'src="./Giambattista_morgagni.gif" width="200"/><figcaption>Giovanni Battista '
             'Morgagni, who made one of the earliest recorded descriptions of emphysema in '
             '1769</figcaption></figure> <p>The name <i>chronic obstructive pulmonary disease</i> '
             'is believed to have first been used in 1965. Previously it has been known by a '
             'number of different names, including <i>chronic obstructive bronchopulmonary '
             'disease</i>, <i>chronic airflow obstruction</i>, <i>chronic obstructive lung '
             'disease</i>, <i>nonspecific chronic pulmonary disease</i>, and <i>diffuse '
             'obstructive pulmonary syndrome</i>. </p><p>The terms <i>emphysema</i> and <i>chronic '
             'bronchitis</i> were formally defined as components of COPD in 1959 at the <a '
             'href="Novartis" title="Novartis">CIBA</a> guest symposium and in 1962 at the <a '
             'href="American_Thoracic_Society" title="American Thoracic Society">American Thoracic '
             'Society</a> Committee meeting on Diagnostic Standards. </p><p>Early descriptions of '
             'probable emphysema began in 1679 by T. Bonet of a condition of "voluminous lungs" '
             'and in 1769 by Giovanni Morgagni of lungs which were "turgid particularly from air". '
             'In 1721 the first drawings of emphysema were made by Ruysh. René Laennec, used the '
             'term <i>emphysema</i> in his book <i>A Treatise on the Diseases of the Chest and of '
             'Mediate Auscultation</i> (1837) to describe lungs that did not collapse when he '
             'opened the chest during an autopsy. He noted that they did not collapse as usual '
             'because they were full of air and the airways were filled with mucus. In 1842, John '
             'Hutchinson invented the <a href="Spirometer" title="Spirometer">spirometer</a>, '
             'which allowed the measurement of <a href="Vital_capacity" title="Vital '
             'capacity">vital capacity</a> of the lungs. However, his spirometer could only '
             'measure volume, not airflow. Tiffeneau and Pinelli in 1947 described the principles '
             'of measuring airflow. </p><p>Air pollution and the increase in cigarette smoking in '
             'Great Britain at the start of the 20th century led to high rates of chronic lung '
             'disease, though it received little attention until the Great Smog of London in '
             'December 1952. This spurred epidemiological research in the United Kingdom, Holland '
             'and elsewhere. In 1953, George L. Waldbott, an American allergist, first described a '
             "new disease he named <i>smoker's respiratory syndrome</i> in the 1953 <i>Journal of "
             'the American Medical Association</i>. This was the first association between tobacco '
             'smoking and chronic respiratory disease. </p><p>Modern treatments were developed '
             'during the second half of the 20th century. Evidence supporting the use of <a '
             'href="Steroid" title="Steroid">steroids</a> in COPD was published in the late 1950s. '
             '<a href="Bronchodilator" title="Bronchodilator">Bronchodilators</a> came into use in '
             'the 1960s following a promising trial of <a href="Isoprenaline" '
             'title="Isoprenaline">isoprenaline</a>. Further bronchodilators, such as short-acting '
             '<a href="Salbutamol" title="Salbutamol">salbutamol</a>, were developed in the 1970s '
             'and the use of <a href="Long-acting_beta-adrenoceptor_agonist" title="Long-acting '
             'beta-adrenoceptor agonist">long-acting bronchodilators</a> began in the mid-1990s. '
             '</p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Society_and_culture">Society and '
             'culture</h2></div> <p>It is generally accepted that COPD is widely underdiagnosed '
             'and many people remain untreated. In the US the <a class="mw-redirect" href="NIH" '
             'title="NIH">NIH</a> has promoted November as <a href="COPD_Awareness_Month" '
             'title="COPD Awareness Month">COPD Awareness Month</a> to be an annual focus on '
             'increasing awareness of the condition. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Economics">Economics</h3></div> <p>Globally, as of 2010, COPD is estimated to '
             'result in economic costs of $2.1\xa0trillion, half of which occurring in the '
             'developing world. Of this total an estimated $1.9\xa0trillion are direct costs such '
             'as medical care, while $0.2\xa0trillion are indirect costs such as missed work. This '
             'is expected to more than double by 2030. In Europe, COPD represents 3% of healthcare '
             'spending. In the United States, costs of the disease were estimated at $50\xa0'
             'billion in 2010, most of which is due to exacerbation. In the United Kingdom this '
             'cost was in 2021 estimated at £3.8 billion annually. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Research">Research</h2></div> <p><a href="Stem-cell_therapy" '
             'title="Stem-cell therapy">Stem-cell therapy</a> using <a '
             'href="Mesenchymal_stem_cell" title="Mesenchymal stem cell">mesenchymal stem '
             'cells</a> was in June 2021 studied in eight <a href="Clinical_trial" title="Clinical '
             'trial">clinical trials</a> had been completed and seventeen were underway. '
             '</p><p>The effectiveness of <a href="Alpha-1_antitrypsin" title="Alpha-1 '
             'antitrypsin">alpha-1 antitrypsin</a> augmentation treatment for people who have '
             'alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency is unclear. </p><p><a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Metabolomic" title="Metabolomic">Metabolomic</a> approaches to diagnosing and '
             'differentiating subtypes of COPD are being studied. </p><p>Research continues into '
             'the use of <a class="mw-redirect" href="Telehealthcare" '
             'title="Telehealthcare">telehealthcare</a> to treat people with COPD when they '
             'experience episodes of shortness of breath; treating people remotely may reduce the '
             "number of emergency-room visits and improve the person's quality of life. "
             '</p><p>American people with COPD and their caregivers consider the following '
             'COPD-related research areas as the most important: family/social/community research, '
             'well-being of people with COPD, curative research, biomedical therapies, policy, and '
             'holistic therapies. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Other_animals">Other animals</h2></div> <p>Chronic obstructive pulmonary disease '
             'may occur in a number of other animals and may be caused by exposure to tobacco '
             'smoke. Most cases of the disease, however, are relatively mild. In horses it is '
             'known as recurrent airway obstruction (RAO) or <i>heaves</i>. RAO can be quite '
             'severe and most often is linked to exposure to common allergens. COPD is also '
             'commonly found in old dogs. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2">\n'
             '\n'
             '=== ARTICLE 2: Pulmonary function testing ===\n'

             'class="mw-page-container"> <div class="mw-page-container-inner"> <div '
             'class="mw-content-container"> <main class="mw-body" id="content"> <header '
             'class="mw-body-header vector-page-titlebar"> <h1 class="firstHeading '
             'mw-first-heading" id="firstHeading"> <span class="mw-page-title-main" '
             'id="openzim-page-title"><span class="mw-page-title-main">Pulmonary function '
             'testing</span></span> </h1> </header> <a id="top"></a> <div '
             'aria-labelledby="firstHeading" class="vector-body '
             've-init-mw-desktopArticleTarget-targetContainer" data-mw-ve-target-container="" '
             'id="bodyContent"> <div class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" dir="ltr" '
             'id="mw-content-text" lang="en"><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" '
             'dir="ltr" lang="en"> <table class="infobox"><tbody><tr><th class="infobox-above" '
             'colspan="2" style="background-color: lightblue; color:inherit;">Pulmonary function '
             'testing</th></tr><tr><td class="infobox-image" colspan="2"><span '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Frameless"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="357" data-file-width="185" decoding="async" height="357" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Body_plethysmograph_box.jpg" width="185"/></span><div '
             'class="infobox-caption">Plethysmograph "body box"</div></td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">MeSH</th><td class="infobox-data"><span '
             'class="reflink nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" '
             'href="https://meshb.nlm.nih.gov/record/ui?ui=D012129" '
             'rel="nofollow">D012129</a></span></td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row"><a href="OPS-301" title="OPS-301">OPS-301 code</a></th><td '
             'class="infobox-data"><a class="external text" '
             'href="http://www.icd-code.de/ops/code/1-71.html" '
             'rel="nofollow">1-71</a></td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row"><a '
             'href="MedlinePlus" title="MedlinePlus">MedlinePlus</a></th><td '
             'class="infobox-data"><span class="reflink nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" '
             'href="https://medlineplus.gov/ency/article/003853.htm" '
             'rel="nofollow">003853</a></span></td></tr></tbody></table> <table '
             'class="infobox"><tbody><tr><td class="infobox-image" colspan="2"><span '
             'class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Frameless"><img class="mw-file-element" '
             'data-file-height="865" data-file-width="1395" decoding="async" height="155" '
             'loading="lazy" src="./Lungvolumes_Updated.png" width="250"/></span></td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">TLC</th><td class="infobox-data">Total lung '
             'capacity: the volume in the lungs at maximal inflation, the sum of VC and '
             'RV.</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row">TV</th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">Tidal volume: that volume of air moved into or out of the lungs '
             'in 1 breath (TV indicates a subdivision of the lung; when tidal volume is precisely '
             'measured, as in gas exchange calculation, the symbol TV or V is '
             'used.)</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row">RV</th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">Residual volume: the volume of air remaining in the lungs after '
             'a maximal exhalation</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row">ERV</th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">Expiratory reserve volume: the maximal volume of air that can '
             'be exhaled from the end-expiratory position</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row">IRV</th><td class="infobox-data">Inspiratory reserve volume: the maximal '
             'volume that can be inhaled from the end-inspiratory level</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">IC</th><td class="infobox-data">Inspiratory '
             'capacity: the sum of IRV and TV</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row">IVC</th><td class="infobox-data">Inspiratory vital capacity: the maximum '
             'volume of air inhaled from the point of maximum expiration</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">VC</th><td class="infobox-data">Vital capacity: '
             'the volume of air breathed out after the deepest inhalation.</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">V</th><td class="infobox-data">Tidal volume: that '
             'volume of air moved into or out of the lungs during quiet breathing (VT indicates a '
             'subdivision of the lung; when tidal volume is precisely measured, as in gas exchange '
             'calculation, the symbol TV or V is used.)</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row">FRC</th><td class="infobox-data">Functional residual capacity: the '
             'volume in the lungs at the end-expiratory position</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">RV/TLC%</th><td class="infobox-data">Residual '
             'volume expressed as percent of TLC</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row">V</th><td class="infobox-data">Alveolar gas volume</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">V</th><td class="infobox-data">Actual volume of '
             'the lung including the volume of the conducting airway.</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">FVC</th><td class="infobox-data">Forced vital '
             'capacity: the determination of the vital capacity from a maximally forced expiratory '
             'effort</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row">FEV</th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">Forced expiratory volume (time): a generic term indicating the '
             'volume of air exhaled under forced conditions in the first <i>t</i> '
             'seconds</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row">FEV</th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">Volume that has been exhaled at the end of the first second of '
             'forced expiration</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row">FEF</th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">Forced expiratory flow related to some portion of the FVC '
             'curve; modifiers refer to amount of FVC already exhaled</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">FEF</th><td class="infobox-data">The maximum '
             'instantaneous flow achieved during a FVC maneuver</td></tr><tr><th '
             'class="infobox-label" scope="row">FIF</th><td class="infobox-data">Forced '
             'inspiratory flow: (Specific measurement of the forced inspiratory curve is denoted '
             'by nomenclature analogous to that for the forced expiratory curve. For example, '
             'maximum inspiratory flow is denoted FIF. Unless otherwise specified, volume '
             'qualifiers indicate the volume inspired from RV at the point of '
             'measurement.)</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" scope="row">PEF</th><td '
             'class="infobox-data">Peak expiratory flow: The highest forced expiratory flow '
             'measured with a peak flow meter</td></tr><tr><th class="infobox-label" '
             'scope="row">MVV</th><td class="infobox-data">Maximal voluntary ventilation: volume '
             'of air expired in a specified period during repetitive maximal '
             'effort</td></tr><tr><td class="infobox-navbar" '
             'colspan="2"></td></tr></tbody></table> <p><b>Pulmonary function testing</b> '
             '(<b>PFT</b>) is a complete evaluation of the <a href="Respiratory_system" '
             'title="Respiratory system">respiratory system</a> including patient history, '
             'physical examinations, and tests of pulmonary function. The primary purpose of '
             'pulmonary function testing is to identify the severity of pulmonary impairment. '
             'Pulmonary function testing has diagnostic and therapeutic roles and helps clinicians '
             'answer some general questions about patients with lung disease. PFTs are normally '
             'performed by a pulmonary function technologist, respiratory therapist, respiratory '
             'physiologist, physiotherapist, <a href="Pulmonology" '
             'title="Pulmonology">pulmonologist</a>, or general practitioner. </p> <meta '
             'property="mw:PageProp/toc"/> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Indications">Indications</h2></div> <p>Pulmonary function testing is a '
             'diagnostic and management tool used for a variety of reasons, such as: </p> '
             '<ul><li>Diagnose lung disease.</li> <li>Monitor the effect of chronic diseases like '
             '<a href="Asthma" title="Asthma">asthma</a>, <a '
             'href="Chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Chronic obstructive pulmonary '
             'disease">chronic obstructive lung disease</a>, or <a href="Cystic_fibrosis" '
             'title="Cystic fibrosis">cystic fibrosis</a>.</li> <li>Detect early changes in lung '
             'function.</li> <li>Identify narrowing in the airways.</li> <li>Evaluate airway '
             'bronchodilator reactivity.</li> <li>Show if environmental factors have harmed the '
             'lungs</li> <li>Preoperative testing</li></ul> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Neuromuscular_disorders">Neuromuscular disorders</h3></div> '
             '<p>Pulmonary function testing in patients with neuromuscular disorders helps to '
             'evaluate the respiratory status of patients at the time of diagnosis, monitor their '
             'progress and course, evaluate them for possible surgery, and gives an overall idea '
             'of the prognosis. </p><p><a href="Duchenne_muscular_dystrophy" title="Duchenne '
             'muscular dystrophy">Duchenne muscular dystrophy</a> is associated with gradual loss '
             'of muscle function over time. Involvement of respiratory muscles results in poor '
             'ability to cough and decreased ability to breathe well and leads to <a '
             'href="Atelectasis" title="Atelectasis">collapse of part or all of the lung</a> '
             'leading to impaired gas exchange and an overall insufficiency in lung strength. </p> '
             '<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Tests">Tests</h2></div> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Spirometry">Spirometry</h3></div> <div '
             'class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Spirometry" title="Spirometry">Spirometry</a></div> <figure '
             'class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><img '
             'class="mw-file-element" data-file-height="1092" data-file-width="1508" '
             'decoding="async" height="181" loading="lazy" src="./DoingSpirometry.JPG" '
             'width="250"/><figcaption>Spirometry</figcaption></figure> <p>Spirometry includes '
             'tests of pulmonary mechanics – measurements of FVC, FEV, FEF values, forced '
             'inspiratory flow rates (FIFs), and MVV. Measuring pulmonary mechanics assesses the '
             'ability of the lungs to move huge volumes of air quickly through the airways to '
             'identify airway obstruction. </p><p>The measurements taken by the spirometry device '
             'are used to generate a pneumotachograph that can help to assess lung conditions such '
             'as: asthma, pulmonary fibrosis, cystic fibrosis, and chronic obstructive pulmonary '
             'disease. <a href="Physician" title="Physician">Physicians</a> may also use the test '
             'results to diagnose bronchial hyperresponsiveness to exercise, cold air, or '
             'pharmaceutical agents. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 '
             'id="Helium_dilution">Helium dilution</h4></div> <div class="hatnote '
             'navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Helium_dilution_technique" title="Helium dilution technique">Helium dilution '
             'technique</a></div> <p>The <a href="Helium_dilution_technique" title="Helium '
             'dilution technique">helium dilution technique</a> for measuring lung volumes uses a '
             'closed, rebreathing circuit. This technique is based on the assumptions that a known '
             'volume and concentration of helium in air begin in the closed <a href="Spirometer" '
             'title="Spirometer">spirometer</a>, that the patient has no helium in their lungs, '
             'and that an equilibration of helium can occur between the spirometer and the lungs. '
             '</p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Nitrogen_washout">Nitrogen '
             'washout</h4></div> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main '
             'article: <a href="Nitrogen_washout" title="Nitrogen washout">Nitrogen '
             'washout</a></div> <p>The <a href="Nitrogen_washout" title="Nitrogen '
             'washout">nitrogen washout</a> technique uses a non-rebreathing open circuit. The '
             'technique is based on the assumptions that the nitrogen concentration in the lungs '
             'is 78% and in equilibrium with the atmosphere, that the patient inhales 100% oxygen '
             'and that the oxygen replaces all of the nitrogen in the lungs. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Plethysmography">Plethysmography</h3></div> '
             '<div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main articles: <a '
             'href="Plethysmograph" title="Plethysmograph">Plethysmograph</a> and <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Lung_volumes" title="Lung volumes">Lung volumes</a></div> '
             "<p>The plethysmography technique applies Boyle's law and uses measurements of volume "
             'and pressure changes to determine total lung volume, assuming temperature is '
             "constant. </p><p>There are four lung volumes and four lung capacities. A lung's "
             'capacity consists of two or more lung volumes. The lung volumes are <a '
             'href="Tidal_volume" title="Tidal volume">tidal volume</a> (V), <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Inspiratory_reserve_volume" title="Inspiratory reserve '
             'volume">inspiratory reserve volume</a> (IRV), <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Expiratory_reserve_volume" title="Expiratory reserve volume">expiratory '
             'reserve volume</a> (ERV), and <a class="mw-redirect" href="Lung_volumes" title="Lung '
             'volumes">residual volume</a> (RV). The four lung capacities are <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Total_lung_capacity" title="Total lung capacity">total '
             'lung capacity</a> (TLC), inspiratory capacity (IC), <a '
             'href="Functional_residual_capacity" title="Functional residual capacity">functional '
             'residual capacity</a> (FRC) and <a href="Vital_capacity" title="Vital '
             'capacity">vital capacity</a> (VC). </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Maximal_respiratory_pressures">Maximal respiratory pressures</h3></div> <div '
             'class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Respiratory_pressure_meter" title="Respiratory pressure meter">Respiratory '
             'pressure meter</a></div> <p>Measurement of maximal inspiratory and expiratory '
             'pressures is indicated whenever there is an unexplained decrease in vital capacity '
             'or respiratory muscle weakness is suspected clinically. Maximal inspiratory pressure '
             '(MIP) is the maximal pressure that can be produced by the patient trying to inhale '
             'through a blocked mouthpiece. Maximal expiratory pressure (MEP) is the maximal '
             'pressure measured during forced expiration (with cheeks bulging) through a blocked '
             'mouthpiece after a full inhalation. Repeated measurements of MIP and MEP are useful '
             'in following the course of patients with <a class="mw-redirect" href="Neuromuscular" '
             'title="Neuromuscular">neuromuscular</a> disorders. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Diffusing_capacity">Diffusing capacity</h3></div> <div '
             'class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" role="note">Main article: <a '
             'href="Diffusing_capacity" title="Diffusing capacity">Diffusing capacity</a></div> '
             '<p>Measurement of the single-breath diffusing capacity for carbon monoxide (DLCO) is '
             'a fast and safe tool in the evaluation of both restrictive and <a '
             'href="Obstructive_lung_disease" title="Obstructive lung disease">obstructive lung '
             'disease</a>. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Bronchodilator_responsiveness">Bronchodilator responsiveness</h3></div> <p>When '
             'a patient has an obstructive defect, a bronchodilator test is given to evaluate if '
             'airway constriction is reversible with a short acting beta-agonist. This is defined '
             'as an increase of ≥12% and ≥200 mL in the FEV1 or FVC. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Oxygen_desaturation_during_exercise">Oxygen desaturation during '
             'exercise</h3></div> <p>The six-minute walk test is a good index of physical function '
             'and therapeutic response in patients with a chronic <a class="mw-redirect" '
             'href="Lung_disease" title="Lung disease">lung disease</a>, such as <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="COPD" title="COPD">COPD</a> or <a '
             'href="Idiopathic_pulmonary_fibrosis" title="Idiopathic pulmonary '
             'fibrosis">idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis</a>. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Arterial_blood_gases">Arterial blood gases</h3></div> <p><a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="Arterial_blood_gas" title="Arterial blood gas">Arterial '
             'blood gases</a> (ABGs) are a helpful measurement in pulmonary function testing in '
             'selected patients. The primary role of measuring ABGs in individuals that are '
             'healthy and stable is to confirm hypoventilation when it is suspected on the basis '
             'of medical history, such as respiratory muscle weakness or advanced <a '
             'class="mw-redirect" href="COPD" title="COPD">COPD</a>. </p><p>ABGs also provide a '
             'more detailed assessment of the severity of hypoxemia in patients who have low '
             'normal oxyhemoglobin saturation. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Risks">Risks</h2></div> <p>Pulmonary function testing is a safe procedure; '
             'however, there is cause for concern regarding untoward reactions and the value of '
             'the test data should be weighed against potential hazards. Some complications '
             'include dizziness, shortness of breath, coughing, pneumothorax, and inducing an '
             'asthma attack. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Contraindications">Contraindications</h2></div> <p>There are some indications '
             'against a pulmonary function test being done. These include a recent heart attack, '
             'stroke, head injury, an aneurysm, or confusion. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading2"><h2 id="Technique">Technique</h2></div> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Preparation">Preparation</h3></div> <p>Subjects have '
             'measurements of height and weight taken before spirometry to determine what their '
             'predicted values should be. Additionally, a history of smoking, recent illness, and '
             'medications is taken. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 '
             'id="Quality_control">Quality control</h3></div> <p>In order for the forced vital '
             'capacity to be considered accurate it has to be conducted three times where the peak '
             'is sharp in the flow-volume curve and the exhalation time is longer than 6 seconds. '
             '</p><p>Repeatability of the PFT is determined by comparing the values of forced '
             'vital capacity (FVC) and forced expiratory volume at 1 second (FEV1). The difference '
             'between the highest values of two FVCs need to be within 5% or 150 mL. When the FVC '
             'is less than 1.0 L, the difference between the highest two values must be within 100 '
             'mL. Lastly, the difference between the two highest values of FEV1 should also be '
             'within 150 mL. The highest FVC and FEV1 may be used from each different test. Until '
             'the results of three tests meet the criteria of reproducibility, the test can be '
             'repeated up to eight times. If it is still not possible to get accurate results, the '
             'best three tests are used. </p> <div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 '
             'id="Clinical_significance">Clinical significance</h2></div> <p>Changes in lung '
             'volumes and capacities from normal are generally consistent with the pattern of lung '
             'impairment. </p><p>Spirometry is required for a diagnosis of COPD. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Interpretation_of_tests">Interpretation of '
             'tests</h2></div> <table class="wikitable" style="float: right"> '
             '<caption>Classification of COPD based on spirometry </caption> <tbody><tr> '
             '<th>Severity </th> <th>FEV1\xa0% predicted </th></tr> <tr> <td>Mild (GOLD 1) </td> '
             '<td>≥80 </td></tr> <tr> <td>Moderate (GOLD 2) </td> <td>50–79 </td></tr> <tr> '
             '<td>Severe (GOLD 3) </td> <td>30–49 </td></tr> <tr> <td>Very severe (GOLD 4) </td> '
             '<td>&lt;30 </td></tr></tbody></table> <div class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" '
             'role="note">See also: <a href="Spirometer#History_-_Interpreting_Spirometry" '
             'title="Spirometer">Spirometer §\xa0History - Interpreting Spirometry</a></div> '
             '<p>Professional societies such as the <a href="American_Thoracic_Society" '
             'title="American Thoracic Society">American Thoracic Society</a> and the <a '
             'href="European_Respiratory_Society" title="European Respiratory Society">European '
             'Respiratory Society</a> have published guidelines regarding the conduct and '
             'interpretation of pulmonary function testing to ensure standardization and '
             'uniformity in performance of tests. The interpretation of tests depends on comparing '
             'the patients values to published normals from previous studies. Deviation from '
             'guidelines can result in false-positive or false negative test results, even though '
             'only a small minority of pulmonary function laboratories followed published '
             'guidelines for spirometry, lung volumes and diffusing capacity in 2012. </p> <div '
             'class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="COPD">COPD</h3></div> <p>The <a '
             'href="Global_Initiative_for_Chronic_Obstructive_Lung_Disease" title="Global '
             'Initiative for Chronic Obstructive Lung Disease">Global Initiative for Chronic '
             'Obstructive Lung Disease</a> provides guidelines for the diagnosis, severity, and '
             'management of <a href="Chronic_obstructive_pulmonary_disease" title="Chronic '
             'obstructive pulmonary disease">COPD</a>. To determine obstruction in a patient\'s '
             'lungs, the post-bronchodilator FEV1/FVC needs to be &lt;0.7. Then, the FEV1 '
             'percentage of predicted result is used to determine the degree of obstruction where '
             'the lower the percent the worse the obstruction. </p> <div class="mw-heading '
             'mw-heading3"><h3 id="Maximum_respiratory_pressures">Maximum respiratory '
             'pressures</h3></div> <p>Several calculations are needed for what a normal maximum '
             'inspiratory (MIP) and expiratory pressure (MEP) is. For males this found by: '
             '</p><p><span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"><span '
             'class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math '
             'alttext="{\\displaystyle MIP=120-(0.41\\times age)}" '
             'xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"> <semantics> <mrow '
             'class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>M</mi> '
             '<mi>I</mi> <mi>P</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>120</mn> <mo>−<!-- − --></mo> <mo '
             'stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0.41</mn> <mo>×<!-- × --></mo> <mi>a</mi> <mi>g</mi> '
             '<mi>e</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation '
             'encoding="application/x-tex">{\\displaystyle MIP=120-(0.41\\times age)}</annotation> '
             '</semantics> </math></span><img alt="{\\displaystyle MIP=120-(0.41\\times age)}" '
             'aria-hidden="true" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" '
